Every Track From ‘Grand Theft Auto VI: The Album’ Ranked — Now Including the Deluxe Bonus Tracks

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

GTA's accompanying soundtrack arrived 24 hours after the long-anticipated Rockstar video game — and they didn't waste time releasing the deluxe edition days later.

Monocultural moments in society have become extremely rare in the mid-2020s, but the arrival of Grand Theft Auto VI has brought about as strong a unified pop-culture tidal wave as we’ve seen this decade.

Rockstar Games released the Grand Theft Auto VI: The Album on Friday (Nov. 20) through Atlantic Records as an accompanying soundtrack to the long-awaited video game, which arrived 24 hours earlier.

Rockstar didn’t spare any expense when piecing together the 34-track album, which features Playboi Carti, Dua Lipa, Cardi B, Lana Del Rey, Gunna, Don Toliver, Kodak Black, Yung Lean, Future, Metro Boomin, Travis Scott, Fred again.., PinkPantheress, Morgan Wallen, Tame Impala, Fuerza Regida and more.

GTA VI will look to make even more history with a No. 1 debut on the charts — as no video game soundtrack has topped the Billboard 200 before.

Artist-hosted radio stations dominate the Vice City airwaves, with stations helmed or curated by the likes of Bad Bunny, Lana Del Rey, Morgan Wallen, Trina and Trick Daddy, PinkPantheress, Burna Boy and Robyn.

The Grand Theft Auto VI festivities will continue into the weekend, with a music festival at Miami’s Mana Wynwood on Nov. 21. The festival is set to have Cardi B, Travis Scott, Rauw Alejandro and more hit the stage, including a Cactus Jack ensemble featuring Scott, Don Toliver, SoFaygo and a cameo from Florida’s own Kodak Black.

After GTA hit pop culture with a tsunami takeover, Rockstar Games and Atlantic Records continued to flood the marketplace with a 23-track deluxe edition of the album, which arrived on Monday (Nov. 23).

Take a break from perusing Leonida with Jason and Lucia and find all of the tracks from the Grand Theft Auto: The Album along with the new deluxe edition (sans the five Overmono songs that feel more like a score) ranked in order below.

Who knew Omar Apollo had a funky little party-starter like this up his sleeve? Drawing on the iconic Stalag riddim immortalized by Sister Nancy’s “Bam Bam,” “No Me Busques” bursts with a wailing chorus with an infectious call-and-response hook. Oh, and that festive saxophone riff. Lyrically, there’s not much to it beyond Apollo’s repeated insistence that someone stop looking for him. The groove does most of the talking. — ISABELA RAYGOZA

This track is a little different from what I’m used to, but I like it. It plays during the opening credits of GTA VI and serves as the opening track on the game’s album. Sonically, it has a heavy ’80s-inspired synth pulse, electro-disco drums and electric piano that make me feel like it could fit perfectly into any season of Stranger Things.

It’s the perfect way to open the game’s soundtrack. The song is about starting from zero and hustling your way toward success, which feels right at home in the world of GTA. It sets the tone for the kind of journey players are about to embark on. — CHRISTOPHER CLAXTON

Sak pase? Kodak Black teams up with DJ Michael Brun and the duo connects with their Haitian roots, as Yak mixes in Haitian Creole while playfully roasting someone’s baldy haircut and rapping about flipping cocaine. — M.S.

GTA is supposed to have something for everyone, and the metalheads make their presence felt with the thrash aggression of Power Trip’s “Mutual Death Warrant.” I’m ready to either run through a wall or fire up Guitar Hero after pressing play thanks to the Dallas band. — M.S.

The Dare’s synth-heavy brand of dance-punk has invaded the mainstream thanks to pop stars like Charli xcx and ADÉLA leaning into and elevating the subgenre. He serves up the robotic “Interesting” here, which will spin on the EDM radio stations around Leonida and get The Dare a set at Space replica The Vault in Vice City. — M.S.

This is an interesting track and an unapologetically raunchy celebration of sexual confidence, which is exactly the kind of energy we can expect from Sexyy Red. Real Dimez and Sexyy lean into their provocative personalities for a playful record that doesn’t take itself too seriously. Even the title “Cooties” plays on the childhood idea that girls have cooties and that boys stay away from them because they’re afraid of infection. Now, the tables have turned and these ladies are having fun with the concept. — C.C.

A dance-house track that makes it clear this duo doesn’t play around — and that anyone can catch a beatdown. — C.C.

Primal percussion, glitchy electronics and unbridled sexual energy collide on Arca’s self-produced “Sandunguea.” The Venezuelan experimentalist delivers a delirious invitation to dance and indulge, peppering her verses with colorful slang from her homeland. Against abrasive beats, her vocals switch between flirtation and feverish demands, breathy moans and fragmented chants. “Nuestra pasarela es la calle” (the street is our runway), she declares. In true Arca fashion, the dance floor becomes a space for uninhibited self-expression, where pleasure and chaos go hand in hand. — I.R.

“All Again” is about missing someone after a relationship has ended, and wondering whether the love, memories and heartbreak were worth it in the end. It captures that feeling of looking back on a relationship and questioning whether you’d put yourself through it all over again. I’m not sure I would. Would you? — C.C.

Rina Sawayama made waves with her assist on Charli xcx’s “Beg for You” in 2022, and the Japanese pop artist/model is back in the mainstream with the brooding “TRDWFE.” I’m going to be hearing Sawayama asking us to “submit” repeatedly when my head hits the pillow tonight — although “TRDWFE” sounds like it was tailor-made more for the likes of HBO’s The Idol than the GTA vibe. — MICHAEL SAPONARA

With Travis Scott all over Grand Theft Auto‘s rollout, it’s only right he reserved a slot for one of Cactus Jack’s offspring. Sofaygo makes the most of his appearance on “Cashout,” a syrupy melodic blend that SahBabii would’ve been a perfect running mate for. — M.S.

It’s clear that 21 Savage will stand ten toes down on his reputation, street credibility and distrust of his opps. Throughout the track, he draws a line between people who talk about violence and those who are actually willing to stand on business. Savage doesn’t want listeners to forget that actions speak louder than words, and if you’re not really about it, he has no time to waste on you. Don’t forget, Twitter fingers will get you nowhere. As he puts it, “We get up and go at all the smoke, you n—as go live.” — C.C.

Metro is back on production for this one. It’s a quick track about flexing, so you already know the deal: money, cars, clothes and relationships. Thug keeps it short and straight to the point, delivering the kind of flex-heavy record that fits right into the GTA lifestyle. Just remember, anything born out of a criminal lifestyle comes with real consequences. — C.C.

“You Move Me” was made for a top-down cruise through Leonida. NEIL FRANCES’ blend of disco and EDM gives a callback to 1980s Vice City, but something that still feels like it could exist in the modern dance world. — M.S.

Young Nudy lives at the intersection of gaming and rap, so Nudy making an appearance on the soundtrack shouldn’t come as much of a surprise. He serves up the sinister “Don Da Da,” which finds him as a kingpin calling shots, while the track could’ve been an offshoot that didn’t make the cut from his Just Doin Me project. — M.S.

Fcukers is back. The NYC duo’s first release since Ö comes with the house-leaning “Only One” and they’re here to start the party and blow out your speakers. If there’s a rave somewhere in Leonida, Fcukers is headlining. Can we get Tinashe to sprinkle her Popstar magic on a remix? Vocalist Shanny Wise even moonlights as a co-host of GTA Radio’s Stockyard FM alongside PinkPantheress and The Dare in the video game. — M.S.

All the baddies to the front, please! “GMA” stands for “Get Money Anthem,” because if you can’t make money in GTA, what the hell can you do? Hope y’all caught that reference. This is an upbeat party anthem celebrating women who make their own money, no matter how they earn it. JT isn’t knocking anybody’s hustle, and at this point, neither am I. Whether you’re stacking cash in the game or in real life, the message is to stay focused on your goals and tune out the criticism. Shoutout to all the ladies working traditional 9-to-5s, earning college degrees, dancing or hustling on the side. We see you! — C.C.

Keith Richards puts his own spin on Jimmy Reed’s 1961 blues classic “Bright Lights, Big City” for Grand Theft Auto VI: The Album. His version takes a different approach from Reed’s original, but Keith sounds right at home here. It’s a fresh take on a blues classic, and one that fits perfectly into the world of GTA. — C.C.

Fame is a hell of a drug, but Denzel Curry and 454 haven’t forgotten where they came from. “FLA Flow” is a tribute to Florida’s music and hip-hop culture, with both artists reflecting on the street history and neighborhoods that shaped them. — C.C.

With GTA returning to Vice City, having Kodak Black involved was a must. He’s even a character playing himself in the video game’s campaign. A Yak and Don Toliver collaboration seems like an odd pairing on its face — then you mix in the chaotic skittering production, and both the artists and listeners are playing catch-up for the duration of “Let’s Rock.” — M.S.

Opium’s HXG and Tana invite Quavo to their rage rap world with “RCKSTRL!VING” and Huncho makes it work with his trap-laden rhymes mixing in with the grungy soundscape. The rapper-rockstar lifestyle plays an integral role in GTA VI‘s chapter three when the scene switches to downtown Vice City. — M.S.

Offset will always be unapologetic, and “I Just Came To Pop It” is no different. Wealth, status, street credibility and a refusal to back down from anyone are all on display. Offset brags about the lifestyle he’s built, from luxury cars to expensive jewelry, while reflecting on his past in the trap and the life he gets to live today. The contrast between his street beginnings and his multimillion-dollar lifestyle is a reminder of just how far he’s come. You earned it, so pop your s—t, Offset! — C.C.

Tee Grizzley is not one to mess with. “IKYFL” is an unapologetic statement about loyalty, street credibility, success and refusing to let anyone question how far he’s come. The track captures Tee’s mentality of protecting his circle, enjoying the rewards of his success and staying ready for anyone who tries to challenge him. This is one of those tracks that makes you feel untouchable when you’re cruising through Vice City. — C.C.

“Got Dat” debuted on Vice City Rap Radio in GTA VI, and lands on streaming services with the album’s deluxe. Pooh Shiesty’s menacing trap-laden rhymes are a perfect fit for GTA, but, unfortunately, Pooh living out that outlaw lifestyle has landed him behind bars for the foreseeable future. — M.S.

Morgan Wallen colors outside the lines of country while leaning on his pop and R&B influences for “Last Thing You Need.” It’s a smooth, laid-back track for fans to throw on for a late-night cruise through the swamp lands of GTA‘s Leonida. — M.S.

Just when you think you’ve settled into the sleek reggaetón groove of “X,” JHAYCO throws a dance-floor curveball. Co-produced by the Puerto Rican star alongside Tainy and COZY, the track finds Jhay assuring a love interest that he’s nothing like her ex, dishing out a stream of suggestive compliments. But the real thrill comes during the bridge, when bright synths and a pulsating electronic beat give way to a euphoric break before the dembow kicks back in. It’s a catchy little club banger that doubles as a reminder of Jhay’s knack for melding reggaetón with EDM. — I.R.

Of course, the GTA team got the legend Robert Glasper to help close out the album! Bringing together bassist Stanley Clarke, drummer Yussef Dayes and pianist Robert Glasper, this track is straight vibes. No lyrics needed, just let the musicians do their thing. Named after Miami’s historic Overtown neighborhood, the track connects the game’s Vice City map to the real-world roots of Miami. A smooth way to bring the album home. — C.C.

Grand Theft Auto VI: The Album wouldn’t be complete without an Afro-fusion track like this. I love the simple, chant-like hook. This might become my new go-to morning affirmation before heading to work or starting a session in GTA. It’s an uplifting song about resilience, bouncing back from setbacks and continuing to reach for greater heights no matter who or what tries to hold you back.

This is the perfect song to play after a mission doesn’t go as planned. Burna is reminding us that no matter the setback or how people treat you, you’re still unstoppable. Nobody is better than you, so keep pushing and get “bigger, bigger, bigger, bigger, hey!” — C.C.

This is baby-making music. Dâm-Funk and The Time’s Jesse Johnson supply the groovy, funky vibes that have been a staple of the GTA series for over two decades. I’m glad the soundtrack composers made room for them here, because Gen Z isn’t going to know what hit them when a new generation of gamers presses play. — M.S.

Grief, trauma and the emotional exhaustion of being unable to escape memories of someone you’ve lost are at the center of HEALTH’s “NIGHTMARE.” The song uses the idea of a nightmare as a metaphor for being trapped in an endless cycle of emotional pain, where the past continues to haunt you. However, by the second half of the track, HEALTH begins shifting toward the idea of waking up, moving forward and learning to separate the past from the present. — C.C.

Tove Lo wasn’t a name I expected to see on the tracklist for GTA VI, but she makes it work and shuts down any detractors who didn’t think she’d belong. “Blood in Your Eyes” is the blaring experimental pop that should be pumping the dance floor at Leonida’s version of the Space and E11EVEN nightclubs in the game. — M.S.

JENNIE is taking another “Power Trip” with this dance-pop track. In JENNIE’s city, there are no limits. This is a celebration of confidence, status and the freedom to do whatever you want without worrying about who approves. She embraces a larger-than-life persona, putting herself at the center of attention while making it clear that she knows her worth. But there’s nothing wrong with a little self-love, so is this really a power trip? — C.C.

If you see Future and Metro Boomin on a track, nine times out of 10, it’s going to be a banger, and this song proves just that. Shoutout to Yung Lean, because I like the track, but I’m not entirely sure if his presence fits. We have Metro handling the production, of course, and Future’s trap-influenced sound just works perfectly over Metro’s production. The two have such a natural chemistry, and this track is another example of why their collaborations rarely miss.– C.C.

Zara Larsson is chasing carefree desire with “Fast Emotion,” and she has no shame about it. She just wants you to “make it reckless, leave [her] breathless, make [her] heart race.” This track is bright, upbeat and full of energy, making it perfect for cruising down Vice City. “Fast Emotion” is all about craving an intense, spontaneous connection and allowing yourself to get swept up in the excitement of the moment. Through imagery of summer nights, physical attraction and speed, Zara captures a romance that feels exhilarating. — C.C.

Outside of Drake, no rapper made more of 2026 than Don Toliver. “TF?” provides the gloomy soundscape where Toliver is at home, compared to “Let’s Rock.” The raunchy tune could’ve been left on the cutting room floor during his OCTANE sessions, but is a worthy addition to the GTA album. — M.S.

A Cherokee loaded with contraband, police sirens blaring and a desperate escape through the city — “Macacoa 2000” opens like a scene straight out of Grand Theft Auto. Over hard-hitting reggaetón produced by Dímelo Ninow, reminiscent of the genre’s gritty 2000s heyday, Rauw Alejandro narrates a chaotic cop chase in a rapid-fire flow. Near the end, the track switches gears into sultrier territory, with the silky R&B vocals that marked his breakthrough. But beneath the bravado is a deeper reverence for reggaetón’s roots. At the beginning, he salutes the genre’s pioneers and reflects on his own longevity: “Se trata de trascender en la música” (It’s about transcending through music). — I.R.

Lana Del Rey shines as Delta Dawne in her role as a co-host of the rebellious Back Country Radio station in GTA. It’s only right she also delivered on the soundtrack, as LDR revels in a tragic romance for her “Crazy for You” ballad. Enter a Jason and Lucia romance scene from the campaign, with Lana providing the accompaniment. — M.S.

“50 Bands” is a braggadocious celebration of money, luxury, sexual confidence and unapologetic competition. The track is packed with personality as Latto and Flo Milli trade witty punchlines back and forth. Both ladies are flexing their looks, money and influence while taking shots at anyone they feel can’t keep up. When Latto and Flo Milli get together, “throw somе racks on it, make it plural.” — C.C.

Fuerza Regida trades corridos for a breezy reggaetón groove on “Suzuki,” a flirtatious, raunchy cut that finds the San Bernardino hitmakers venturing into new territory. Produced by Synthetic, the track glides on glossy electronic textures and a laid-back dembow bounce, while frontman Jesús Ortiz Paz delivers a string of shameless come-ons. From pole dancing and risqué fantasies to a cheeky reference to tusi (a.k.a. pink cocaine), the lyrics leave little to the imagination. The beat may be easygoing, but JOP clearly has no intention of behaving himself. — I.R.

“Aprovecha que tienes mi atención!” Anitta brings “Touch & Go” to life with this English- and Spanish-language version of “Vai Dar Caô,” originally featuring Ebony and Papatinho. Unfortunately, this version is missing those features — but Anitta still delivers the energy when it comes to casual attraction, sexual freedom and knowing how to leave someone wanting more.

On “Touch & Go,” Anitta enjoys the physical chemistry of a relationship, but has no interest in turning it into a deeper emotional commitment. And that sounds like a lot of y’all reading this! You get close, enjoy the moment and move on before the other person starts expecting anything more. A hot, flirtatious atmosphere can be fun for a while, but there’s no shame in wanting something more serious either. — C.C.

If half of Camila Cabello’s heart is in Havana, the other half lies in Miami. The dulcet “Sweet Like Candy” is a solid effort from CC — and like a good piece of candy, it’s not too heavy or sour, as the Fifth Harmony solo star sticks the landing with a fun, surface-level pop song. It also makes me think about where she has been in the last two years, with Cabello largely out of the pop scene since 2024’s C,XOXO. Time for a comeback, Camila. — M.S.

“Everybody getting’ loose!” Produced by Guy-Manuel de Homem-Christo, Travis definitely found his pocket on this one. I can see this track growing in popularity over time, and it’s already one of my favorites on the album. You have to crank the volume all the way up for this one. There’s so much electronic-infused chaos packed into the production, making it feel like hip-hop built for the club. It fits flawlessly into the GTA universe. — C.C.

Gunna turned back the clock to the days long before he was running 5Ks and flexed a six-pack as a gym rat. The Atlanta native reunites with frequent collaborator Turbo on the production side for a hard-hitting trap banger that boasts a flow that will sound familiar to early Gunna fans from the days of his Drip or Drown and Drip Season series, when he was still rising through the rap ranks. — M.S.

For all the tenderness with which Kali Uchis and Helado Negro sing to each other, their message couldn’t be more blunt: lárgate (or “f–k off”). The Colombian American singer and Ecuadorian American musician explore the emotional contradictions of a relationship that’s run its course, alternating between wistful reflections and outright dismissal. The sweetness of their vocal interplay almost belies the bitterness of their words. — I.R.

Cardi B will always pop her s—t, and “Don’t Chart” is no different. From the Bronx all the way to Vice City, Cardi is talking her talk! The track marks her second release of 2026, following the arrival of “AH HA” in July. This is Cardi through and through: she’s “still poppin’, still winnin’, [and] you still hatin’.” The bouncy production is packed with flex after flex, touching on confidence, wealth, chart success and designer labels. Thank you, Cardi, for another boastful tongue-in-cheek anthem. — C.C.

“INTERNET $TAR” is a celebration of internet fame, individuality and the freedom to embrace who you are, even when the world doesn’t fully understand or appreciate you. Slayyyter presents herself as influential, memorable and impossible to replicate — which, if you know her, is exactly the point. — C.C.

I imagine Playboi Carti has thousands of songs in a folder on hard drives that he could lend out when needed. Whether it was Carti himself or right-hand producer F1LTHY, they made the right choice going with “Ahh Yea.” For Carti’s standards, “Ahh Yea” leans more into the poppier side of his artistry, and could easily be seen playing on the GTA radio while gamers cruise around Leonida. “Ahh Yea” seems like a holdover from the MUSIC era, and I wish we got more loosies like this from King Vamp. — M.S.

Argentine duo CA7RIEL & Paco Amoroso bring their irreverence to the dance floor on “Sexy Magic,” a bouncy, seductive electronic collaboration with British pop artist PinkPantheress, producer Fred again.. and French house veteran Étienne de Crécy. Riding a groovy bassline and shimmering synths, the track pairs Paco’s playful Spanish delivery with PinkPantheress’ breathy English vocals. Psychedelic around the edges and funky at its core, “Sexy Magic” sounds tailor-made for a neon-lit night cruising through Vice City. — I.R.

Travis Scott has been part of the Grand Theft Auto VI rollout every step of the way. Between attending events and making Cactus Jack x GTA merchandise collections, nobody’s been more hyped in hip-hop for the return of the iconic video game series than La Flame.

Scott makes a second appearance on the soundtrack with “Burn This S–t Down,” which sounds as if it has been the most creatively inspired he’s been in the last couple of years. It’s giving me that feeling of a hungry Cactus Jack in the mid-2010s, when he was pushing the boundaries of the genre and making his melodic rage part of hip-hop’s mainstream. That’s a good sign of things to come for La Flame, who should be in album mode for 2027.

“Burn” is a three-part rollercoaster, which he’s experimented with in the past (“SICKO MODE”). Scott plays with melody and then pushes the pace to bar up before turning the title into an eerie chant with an expansive sound that could score a film scene. The final portion will sound familiar to GTA VI gamers, as it was debuted during the explosive final mission of chapter one. — M.S.

New York is in the building! That opening line in the first verse, “You look prettier in person,” hits in this social media era. We all know what it’s like to meet someone in person after finding them online and being happy that they actually look like their pictures — or, in this case, look even better. Boogie, you did your thing with this one, my boy. This is about to go platinum in the crib: “I don’t wanna fight, I wanna dance tonight” — a perfect track for a late-night drive through Vice City.— C.C.

Dua Lipa has proven to be the queen of a soundtrack when she dished out the top 10 Hot 100 bop “Dance the Night” for Barbie in 2023. With an intro that seems to draw from Phil Collins’ “Don’t Lose My Number” and later interpolates KISS’ “I Was Made for Lovin’ You,” the pop star has a penchant for taking songs of yesteryear and making them digestible for today’s audiences. “Go Easy” is no different for the hitmaker, as Dua clears the decks and gears up to launch a new era in the first quarter of ’27. — M.S.

“Flower in a Hurricane” is the preeminent song from GTA VI. In a short time, it’s already essentially become the video game’s theme song, providing the soundtrack to key scenes, clips and compilations going viral on social media. Justice meshes its EDM roots with King Gizzard & the Lizard Wizard’s rock while SZA and Kevin Parker join forces to sing about surviving the storm to make it out alive on the other side. It perfectly balances the angst of everything falling apart at any moment on the journey to Vice City glory. — M.S.

Figma employee sues, alleging company forced her to confront harassers

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A Figma engineer is suing the design software company and its co-founder and CEO, Dylan Field, over allegations that she faced sexual harassment and retaliation while managers and HR repeatedly told her to confront the very people she accused of misconduct.

The lawsuit, filed Monday in U.S. District Court for the Southern District of New York, alleges that Figma’s culture of “direct feedback” led managers and HR to make engineer Serry Park address harassment herself rather than investigate her complaints. Park, who joined Figma in 2022, alleges the company’s response damaged her career, compensation, and mental health. The lawsuit also alleges that Park, who is Asian, faced racial and gender discrimination, including performance criticism based on stereotypes that Asian women are meek, timid, and conflict-averse.

“Figma was my dream company for a long time,” Park told TechCrunch in an interview before the lawsuit was filed. “It was a place where I felt that I could level up in my career, working on a lot of really cool, interesting things at scale.”

Park is seeking damages, a jury trial, and a court order prohibiting Figma from requiring employees who report harassment to confront their alleged harassers. She also wants the company to adopt and enforce a process for investigating harassment complaints promptly and confidentially.

When reached for comment, a spokesperson for Figma said, “Figma does not tolerate discrimination or harassment. Figma also does not tolerate extortion. These claims have no merit, and we will present the facts in court.”

Park told TechCrunch she initially had faith in Figma’s complaint process, expecting that if she documented the alleged harassment and reported it to the right people, the company would act. But as an engineer in a male-dominated field, Park told TechCrunch, “I don’t feel that they took me seriously throughout this process.”

Park, who is still employed at Figma, joined its San Francisco office in October 2022. Within weeks, her onboarding mentor — a fellow engineer — asked her on a date and continued pursuing her after she declined, Park alleges. She initially feared reporting him, worrying that her manager would “dismiss her reports or view her as the problem,” according to the lawsuit.

Park says she tried to avoid her onboarding mentor. In early 2023, for example, she delayed choosing a work project until he had picked one. When her manager criticized her for not being a “team player,” Park explained that she was trying to avoid the colleague she accused of harassment, according to the suit.

Her manager allegedly dismissed her concerns, saying he had “met his wife at work too” and treating the behavior as “harmless flirtation.” When Park asked to be kept off projects with the onboarding mentor, HR allegedly instructed her to confront him directly about his behavior.

The lawsuit ties that response to Figma’s culture of direct feedback, which Park alleges Field established and enforced. It says the company’s People organization trains managers to direct employees, including those reporting harassment, to give feedback directly to the person involved.

In March 2023, her manager assigned him to a major project she was leading, despite her request to be separated from him. Park told her manager that she would “figure it out,” but alleges that having to work with him affected her performance. Her manager gave her a 2 out of 5 on her performance review, criticizing her for not speaking up enough or engaging with others and for lacking presence. Park was put on a coaching plan, which she completed in late 2023.

Changing managers didn’t help, according to the lawsuit. Her second manager also refused to separate them, allegedly telling Park that someone at her level should be able to resolve conflicts with teammates and that separating them would be “avoiding the problem.” Like her previous manager, he allegedly pointed out that he had met his partner at work.

Park alleges the harassment continued throughout 2023.

In April 2024, she transferred to another team, a move she claims set back her career by resetting her performance and promotion timeline.

On her new team, which had more women, Park said she felt better and no longer had to work with her alleged harasser. Her performance reviews improved, but in late 2025, she alleges another incident of sexual harassment, this time by her new manager.

The incident followed a night out for drinks with her wife and two teammates, including her manager. The next day, during a team lunch, the manager allegedly said Park’s sex life had been discussed the previous evening. Park, who said no such conversation had occurred in her or her wife’s presence, felt “horrified and humiliated” by comments she believed fetishized queer relationships.

A colleague reported the comments to HR as sexual harassment, according to the suit. Weeks later, Park’s manager apologized in a Slack message, saying she didn’t recall what she had said. When Park sought HR’s guidance, she was again told to address the matter directly with her manager.

During their conversation, the manager allegedly became defensive, and the encounter left Park feeling “crushed, anxious, and vulnerable.” The manager also allegedly said she would never make such a comment because she had “a lot of gay friends.”

The manager told HR that they had agreed it was a misunderstanding. HR closed its investigation without interviewing witnesses, according to the suit, even though Park disputed her manager’s account.

“Requiring an employee to confront her harasser as the price of complaining — and then closing her complaint — would dissuade a reasonable employee from complaining at all,” the suit said.

Park alleges her manager retaliated by publicly berating her, sometimes so aggressively that colleagues intervened or checked on her afterward, according to the lawsuit. The suit also alleges that other teammates left the team over concerns about the manager’s behavior.

Park again complained to HR about “escalating retaliation” and expressed concern the manager would “derail her career.” HR allegedly defended the manager’s conduct, saying the manager was “within her reasonable authority” and again instructed Park to address her concerns directly with the manager.

In early 2026, Park received another low performance review and a reduced bonus. The lawsuit alleges that similarly similarly situated men continued to receive promotions, raises, and bonuses. When Park reported the negative performance review to HR in March, she was allegedly told her sexual harassment complaint against her manager could not be “substantiated.”

Park took medical leave in March to receive treatment for PTSD symptoms and generalized anxiety. Cleared to return to work in June, she was prescribed workplace accommodations, including a graduated work schedule and mandatory remote workdays.

Park told TechCrunch that Figma approved the accommodations but had not implemented them at the time of the interview.  In July, Park and her lawyers notified Figma of their intent to pursue legal action.

“It’s really scary for me,” she said about taking legal action. “I don’t know what’s going to happen next with this, I don’t know what the outcome is going to be, but at least it feels like this is my chance to do the right thing.”

Trump Hits New Low With Bizarre Nvidia CEO Meme

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The president shared an image featuring the philandering businessman in Trump’s own pose after the attempted assassination in Butler, Pennsylvania.

The news that Jensen Huang has been reinstated as the CEO of the world’s fourth most valuable company has been met with both applause from investors and derision and confusion from those who thought the leather-jacket-clad adulterer was being dragged to the trash bin. His return to Nvidia was facilitated by Vice President JD Vance and celebrated by the White House’s social media influencer-in-chief, President Donald J. Trump, with a post on Truth Social, which is hardly truthful, quite anti-social, and rife with the AI slop that has come to characterize the current lame-duck à l'orange administration.

As news of the Huang turn by the Nvidia board caused gasps the world over, including among Congressional Republicans who decried Trump’s involvement in the decision to bring the philanderer back into the fold, Trump shared a meme watermarked by a well-known right-wing meme brand, @GRANDOLDMEMES. In it, the businessman mimics Trump’s own pose after the attempted assassination in Butler, Pennsylvania, as captured by AP photojournalist Evan Vucci, but in Huang’s hand is a poorly AI-generated — or is it photoshopped? — brassiere as stiff as it is bright. It seems to say: Sure, the man is a lech, but he has dodged a bullet, even if blood is smeared across his face. The American flag is replaced with the corporate logo, and hell, considering the way the government is being run, a corporate takeover of this nation doesn’t seem all that outlandish if you think about it.

But what does the red mean? In Nathaniel Hawthorne's 19th-century novel, the titular scarlet letter marks an adulterer's public shame, but here the echo of Trump's 2024 comeback points to redemption. There’s another reading, because it was reported that the billionaire, now apparently worth $144.1 billion since the stock jumped 31% at the news of his reappointment, slept not only with a subordinate but also with a woman who is allegedly a Chinese spy, though Attorney General Todd Blanche has hedged on that claim. Is it a wink to the red of the Chinese flag?

We have come to expect so little from Trump and his edgelord memesters, but this is just truly and utterly bizarre. Perhaps the man at the helm of the world’s most powerful country thought it was funny that Huang is being ushered away by a blonde Secret Service agent who hugs his torso, or he sees himself in the once disgraced and now restored businessman — very likely.

The phrase “let your freak flag fly” has now lost all meaning when billionaires brazenly act like they’re above the law and a call to the president can make all the bad things go away. The man who makes the chips to churn out slop is now rendered in a mock-heroic pose. Once we immortalized the powerful in paint; nowadays we humor them with pixels.

The Center of the News Universe Has Shifted to CNBC

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The most consequential stories of this moment in time are happening on the Versant brand, from the stunning fall and rise of Nvidia's Jensen Huang, to Anthropic's IPO.

On Monday morning, CNBC senior Washington correspondent Eamon Javers was driving in his car when the President of the United States called.

Javers pulled over to the side of the road, and within minutes he and President Trump were live on Squawk on the Street to talk about Nvidia‘s shock decision to bring back CEO Jensen Huang. Trump was on the show for nearly an hour, revealing his central role in securing Huang’s return.

In an interview with The Hollywood Reporter Monday afternoon, KC Sullivan, the president of CNBC, called this morning’s coverage of Huang and Nvidia a “really special moment.”

“The President was so generous with his time, but it was fun to see Eamon bring that in, and then to have [Jim] Cramer jump in, Sarah Eisen jump in, Carl Quintanilla jump in, David Faber jump in, and to be able to get all those different perspectives, I just think it made such a rich discussion,” Sullivan said. “I was really just so proud of the team and how they delivered.”

For months, the news business has been focused on the 2026 midterm elections, and for good reason. But the midterms are over, and suddenly it seems the most consequential stories of this moment in time are happening on CNBC.

Nvidia stock soared 31.8 percent on Monday after the news, and the entire stock market rallied. Huang and Nvidia are not only a business story, they are an economic story, a political story, a national security story. It’s a story of our time. For CNBC, it was also the second highest hour of web traffic this year, behind only SpaceX’s IPO in June.

“In the world of media now, it’s those who are essential to their audience and really understand their brand promise and deliver on that are the ones that are going to going to thrive,” Sullivan says. “I do think that there’s a lot of attention on the business world right now, and I don’t think there’s anybody who can compete with us in that arena.”

For CNBC, that audience includes Trump, as well as seemingly every CEO of note in America. “I want to congratulate you guys. You covered it really well,” Trump told the Squawk on the Street hosts during his marathon interview.

Huang, of course, is just the latest news. Last week, it was Cramer’s exclusive interview with Nvidia’s interim Colette Kress as the company reported its first (and only?) earnings report without Huang at the helm. Two days later Andrew Ross Sorkin had an exclusive interview with Anthropic co-founders Dario and Daniela Amodei as the AI company IPOd.

“It’s not like those things just happen. Those are long-term, trusted relationships,” Sullivan explains. Eamon has been working that relationship to the President for many months, which put him in a position to do that; Cramer’s relationship and trust with Nvidia is what allowed him to get that first interview with Colette; [Joe] Kernen’s got a really important, longstanding relationship with the Treasury Secretary so when he was playing a major role over the last two weeks, to be able to have him come on our air; Sorkin has the relationship into Anthropic. Those aren’t gimmes. Those aren’t just things that happen.”

Those moments all delivered double-digit ratings increases for CNBC on linear TV, and secured millions of social views and engagements, where they were shared, analyzed and moved the markets.

“At CNBC we pride ourselves on this, but we’re the place that the most influential people go to at the most important times and most important moments,” Sullivan says. “I think what it comes down to is that our audience wants to understand what is changing, what does it mean, and what do they do with that information? And that’s everything from the midterms to the AI trade. When they’re trying to to figure out what’s going on, they turn to us. And I think our guests know that as well.”

The TV news business can be fickle. General interest brands like CNN and NBC News can benefit immensely when major global news is happening, but tend to be consistent on a day-to-day basis. More partisan-leaning cable news channels like MS NOW and Fox News can rise and fall with the outcome of elections, or how people on the right or left side of the spectrum are feeling.

In the case of CNBC, the news cycle today is simply in their wheelhouse, and it is poised to continue over time as OpenAI weighs its own IPO, and with investigations into Nvidia assuredly coming from Congress (MS NOW may benefit from those too).

The CNBC boom also comes at a good time for the news brand, which is set to launch a new direct-to-consumer offering in the coming months. Sullivan is coy on what that specific offering will entail, but he notes that it will lean into its brand attributes of trust, and the live exclusives that the channel has become known for, be it an exclusive interview with the Amodei siblings, or having the President on the phone for nearly an hour.

“I think that what you’ll see when we roll out our direct-to-consumer product early next year is that we’re doubling down on that to deliver for the audience where and how they want it, making CNBC more useful and making CNBC easier to interact with and more rich for the consumer where and how they want it, but all built off of the same tenets that you saw today or over the last few weeks.”

China's Z.ai Says Its New AI Model Can Think Like One Four Times Its Size

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

China's Z.ai Says Its New AI Model Can Think Like One Four Times Its Size

Chinese AI developer Z.ai released GLM-5.4 on Monday, introducing a new architecture that it claims delivers the reasoning capabilities of models nearly four times its size without requiring nearly as many parameters.

The Beijing-based company says its new 750-billion-parameter model can compete with systems containing two to three trillion parameters. It also supports a one-million-token context window, accepts images and video, and can generate up to 128,000 tokens in a single response, according to its technical documentation. Parameters are the internal values a model learns during training, while tokens are the chunks of text it processes and generates—roughly equivalent to words or parts of words.

The release comes as American companies including OpenAI, Anthropic, and Google dominate the highest positions in AI performance rankings. Chinese developers, meanwhile, have increasingly focused on making powerful models cheaper to run, particularly for coding and autonomous agents.

You can now act on the news you read: Go long or short Anthropic with perpetual futures, all from your Decrypt Money Account.

Z.ai has been among China's strongest competitors. As Decrypt reported in August, its predecessor GLM-5.3 was already competitive with leading American coding models, although it still trailed the best proprietary systems on several software engineering tests.

GLM-5.4 introduces what Z.ai calls a Loop Transformer, which processes information through the same 28 neural network layers twice. Traditional Transformers generally pass information through each layer once, so reusing layers effectively increases computational depth without increasing the number of stored parameters.

The company combines this approach with gated updates, which regulate how much information changes between passes, and a hybrid memory cache that avoids storing an entirely separate copy of previous calculations. Sparse attention also helps the model focus its computing power on relevant information rather than processing everything equally.

In controlled experiments on a smaller 30-billion-parameter model, Z.ai reported that its looping technique increased accuracy on AIME 2025, a mathematics examination benchmark scored by the percentage of correctly solved problems, from 19.17% to 30.42%.

GPQA Diamond, a graduate-level science question-answering test also scored by correct-answer percentage, improved from 47.58% to 56.26%. The experiments required approximately 10% additional training compute, while reducing generated reasoning tokens by 10% to 14% on several tests.

Those results concern the experimental architecture, not independently established performance for GLM-5.4 itself. Z.ai has not published a comprehensive flagship benchmark comparison demonstrating that the new model matches Claude or GPT.

The company is also promoting what it calls Generalized Coding: using programming to accomplish tasks beyond software development. Its demonstrations include building a video game, creating 3D environments, and designing a six-axis robotic arm using engineering software.

The company's previous flagship costs $1.40 per million input tokens and $4.40 per million output tokens through its API. By comparison, OpenAI's GPT-6.2 Sol costs $2 and $10, while Anthropic's Claude Opus 5.5 costs $4 and $20, respectively.

At those published rates, GLM-5.3 is considerably cheaper than its American counterparts, although GLM-5.4's own API pricing has not been announced. Its actual performance-per-dollar advantage therefore cannot yet be calculated.

China has other serious contenders. Alibaba's Qwen 4 Max recently topped Chinese competitors on Arena's agent leaderboard, while Moonshot's Kimi K3 and DeepSeek V4 have challenged American developers on coding performance and API pricing.

The performance gap remains contested. A U.S. government evaluation placed DeepSeek roughly eight months behind the American frontier, although researchers questioned its methodology.

Z.ai itself remains on the U.S. Commerce Department's Entity List, following its January 2025 designation over concerns that its AI research could support Chinese military modernization. The restrictions limit access to controlled American technology, but have not stopped the company from releasing new models.

Hands-on: Baseus’ new PicoGo Pro USB-C charger is clever, cute, and on sale

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

USB-C chargers are pretty standardized at this point, but there’s a fun new trend of adding displays to your power brick that I’ve been very much enjoying, and the new PicoGo Pro from Baseus is a great example with three ports, optimized charging, and a cute little character to top it off – and for Black Friday, it’s pretty deeply discounted, too.

The Baseus PicoGo Pro (AH22) is a 100W, 3-port USB-C charger designed to top off your laptop, phone, and/or tablet, even all at the same time. For anyone who travels often or just has a lot of devices on hand, chargers like this are a must. Having something you don’t have to think twice about when it comes to charging rates is just so nice, and these have come a long way, too.

Using GaN tech, PicoGo Pro is very compact at about 2 inches tall and a little over an inch wide. I remember having single-port chargers that could barely fast-charge my phone that were twice this size just a few years ago. GaN just absolutely rules, and Baseus is using a special version that reduces heat and improves efficiency.

But the two reasons I really like this charger aren’t strictly to do with GaN.

The first is the built-in display. Again, that’s a trend throughout the industry. Anker has several chargers out with built-in displays as the company helped to really make this popular – the Anker Prime 160W brick is really similar to PicoGo Pro (at a higher price), and it’s the only I’ve been using for months before trying out this Baseus model.

Baseus has a cute little animated character that accompanies your charging, with little eyes when the charger isn’t active and some fun animations to show your charging rate in real-time. Neat! You can “press” the button to move through the UI and even see the specific charging rate of each connected device. You’ll also see that the brick can recognize the device you have plugged in, though Android devices just show a generic “SAMSUNG” or whatever brand you plug in – Anker, again, had a similar set of features in a brick released earlier in 2026, but some of those features were iPhone-only, and that brick was only a 1-port, 45W charger anyway.

But, like that Anker brick, Baseus supports “smart” charging that adjusts to your device. Plugging in a Galaxy Z Fold 8 that was around 65% full, I saw the brick adjust its charging speed to tailor to the phone, initially going up to full speed before settling down around 28W~. You can manually adjust this with “Turbo” or “Gentle” modes, the former maxing out the charging rate and the latter settling in around 15W for a slower charge. That flexibility is great because it means you can adjust the charging rate to what you need in that moment. Full-speed for a quick top-off on a busy day, slower charging overnight to preserve battery health.

Baseus isn’t doing much new here, though the GaN tech mentioned earlier is enhanced by some architecture and algorithm adjustments the company has made behind the scenes, but it’s a really good implementation.

Beyond that, it’s also priced pretty well.

Baseus PicoGo Pro’s full price is $59.99, but you’ll probably rarely see it there. Technically, today is its launch date, and it’s already down by over 30% at $39.70 for Black Friday. Value-wise, this one is hard to beat.

The 6 Most Outrageous GTA 6 Myths, Debunked

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The internet is already drowning in GTA 6 misinformation

After a wild opening weekend, Grand Theft Auto 6 is off to the races. Millions are playing Rockstar’s enormous open-world game and discovering its complexities in real time. There’s a lot to unpack in a game that’s filled with intersecting gameplay systems across a long story, and some players are already taking advantage of that. At the same time, the opening days of GTA 6’s launch have been filled with misinformation, much of it fueled by culture warriors and content creators looking to turn outrage into clicks on social media platforms that incentivize such behavior.

Because of that, there are a lot of myths about GTA 6 currently floating around the internet. Some come from harmless misunderstandings, while others are more deliberate misrepresentations intended to scare off potential players. To help you navigate the noise, we’ve rounded up some of the most rampant myths currently circulating around GTA 6 and fact-checked them accordingly.

The claim: GTA 6’s story forces you to do chores for Lucia instead of crimes.

If you spent launch day watching reactions to GTA 6’s early hours roll in instead of playing, you might have walked away thinking that Rockstar made a chore simulator. That’s thanks to a few early missions that revolve around Jason getting his home ready for Lucia as she’s about to be released from prison. Players will paint a bedroom for her, fix a leaky roof, and repair some stairs. There’s also an optional quest where players can run errands to prepare for Lucia's arrival, like picking up groceries or buying her a necklace. All of this happens in the first few hours of the game. GTA 6 quickly ramps up after that, getting into the series’ usual flow of cop chases, heists, and shootouts. You can call it a slow start, but know that any mundane missions you’ve seen make up a very small amount of story that you’re likely to see in your very first play session.

The claim: GTA 6 doesn’t allow you to steal cars, or heavily restricts what you can steal.

While there’s a grain of truth to this, it has been heavily exaggerated on social media. There are some select cars that you can’t steal in GTA 6, like those around Jason’s neighborhood or in an NPC’s car lot. But for the most part, you can jack just about any vehicle you see in Leonida. The catch is that you’ll need a device that can copy keys to get into some of the more expensive cars, and you won’t get that until Chapter 3. Being able to steal anything requires some patience as you play through the story, but be rest assured that you can do as much grand theft auto as ever in Grand Theft Auto.

The claim: Airplanes will bounce off of buildings instead of exploding on impact.

This one is a little tricky to unpack because there seem to be two weird things colliding here. After videos of airplanes bouncing off of skyscrapers like rubber began to emerge on social media, players deduced that content creators were sharing videos where they had their Reduced Vehicle Damage setting turned on. It seemed like a textbook case of outrage farming, though there may be a slight bit of truth here. Some players have speculated that GTA 6 may have a few physics bugs that make it tough for certain airliners to blow up as expected. That may be an edge case, but I can confirm that I have crashed plenty of planes into buildings in GTA 6, and they have all exploded into a fiery wreck so far.

The claim: GTA 6 features no autosave, and you will lose progress if you don’t manually save.

When players started reporting that they were losing hours of gameplay because GTA 6 didn’t have an autosave feature, I was very confused. I can open my save file right now and see a dedicated auto save slot that is regularly refreshed as I play. I initially wrote it off as players either lying or not realizing that the auto save only triggers after completing major missions, but it turns out they weren’t hallucinating. GTA 6’s first patch fixed an issue where auto save wasn’t triggering properly. So, yes, GTA 6 does have autosave; it just wasn’t working correctly for some players at launch. It shouldn’t be a problem now, but always remember to save early and often just in case.

The claim: Lucia is cheating on Jason during the story.

This one is downright bizarre. One of the first talking points that came out about GTA 6 was that Jason is a “cuck” who does not realize that Lucia is cheating on him. As someone who has played through the story, I spent days trying to figure out where in the world that blatant lie could be coming from. It turns out it was a good old misinterpretation. Early in the story, Lucia sends a text to an unknown character, which she rushes to hide from Jason when he enters the scene. While some have interpreted that as Lucia having a secret lover, it actually turns out that she was texting a friend from prison. To players’ credit, the scene does feel like it’s built to make you think Lucia is hiding a big secret from Jason, so that reaction might have been exactly what Rockstar was hoping to provoke.

The claim: GTA 6 does not allow you to open fire and kill people in a gay club.

The ugliest rumor to come out of GTA 6 so far is that the game will not allow you to walk into a gay club in Vice City and kill people. There was even a clip going around social media that seemed to support that, sparking some grotesque reactions. The truth is that the location in the video isn’t specifically a gay club. It is a club, and it’s true that you can’t open fire in it, but that’s only because you can’t shoot inside any establishment in GTA 6. This claim would technically be true if there was a gay club in the game, but only as a side effect of the wider systems working the way they do. What is true, though, is that the game features an unkillable non-binary character, according to the actor who portrayed them.

This isn't a moral or ethical issue — boringly, it's about regulation

Ma Kolo Muani chi l'ha voluto alla Juventus? Spalletti fa il Conte per evitare che finisca come all'Inter: uno scambio gli toglierebbe ogni alibi

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

La Juventus in estate ha speso 41 milioni di euro più dodici di bonus per acquistare a titolo definitivo Randal Kolo Muani. Ha definito l'operazione dopo una telenovela durata oltre un anno, dopo aver già testato il calciatore a Torino nella stagione 2024/25. Non solo: sempre la scorsa estate in una campagna trasferimenti da oltre 120 milioni di acquisti ne ha investiti 16 per acquistare Jeff Ekhator del Genoa e circa tre per il prestito di Nick Woltemade. La società bianconera ha definito queste tre operazioni con l'intenzione di mettere a disposizione di Luciano Spalletti un nuovo centravanti, l'ha fatto perché Jonathan David - altro numero 9 - non era ritenuto adatto alla Juventus del manager di Certaldo. E non lo era nemmeno Lois Openda. L'unico centravanti 'giusto' per Spalletti era Dusan Vlahovic, calciatore volato a Istanbul perché alla Juve voleva guadagnare più di tutti gli altri nonostante un rendimento non all'altezza degli 80 milioni spesi. Che al Besiktas guadagna 8 milioni più bonus dopo aver incassato un bonus alla firma da dieci milioni di euro.

E gli altri? E gli ultimi tre arrivati? Nessuno è adatto: "Abbiamo bisogno di un centravanti perché non l'abbiamo comprato. Kolo Muani lo rimetterò lì perché serve qualcuno che giochi in quella posizione: nel calcio d'oggi serve una punta fisica che sappia giocare spalle alla porta. Poi è inutile parlare di Spalletti via. C'è bisogno di gente fisica, che riceva la pallata addosso e sappia giocare con le mani addosso del centrale avversario. Ora è così. Si vince i duelli. Ci vuole gente fisica che vinca i duelli ed esca dalle mini prigioni che ci sono in campo", le parole di Spalletti dopo la larga vittoria contro il Venezia.

Ma allora chi ha avallato l'acquisto di Kolo Muani e degli altri centravanti? E' possibile che la Juventus abbia speso metà del suo budget estivo per tre centravanti che non erano graditi al suo allenatore? Evidentemente no, anche perché in estate la società bianconera faceva sapere di definire tutte le operazioni dopo un confronto con l'allenatore. E lo stesso Spalletti - dopo aver fatto cadere dalla torre Damien Comolli - esprimeva soddisfazione per il lavoro portato avanti braccio a braccio col nuovo amministratore delegato Giovanni Carnevali.

E allora cosa c'è dietro? Cosa sta succedendo? La verità è che le parole di Antonio Conte non hanno scosso solo l'ambiente bianconero, ma anche lo stesso manager di Certaldo che ha il timore con questa stagione di preparare il terreno al manager leccese proprio come fece all'Inter. Che sabato pomeriggio ha replicato così alla bussata del manager leccese alla porta bianconera: "Io quando non allenavo studiavo gli altri tecnici con rispetto". Un titolo che non è passato inosservato, una conferenza stampa a cui ne ha fatto seguito un'altra in cui Spalletti - proprio come avrebbe fatto Conte - ha esplicitamente chiesto un acquisto per gennaio. Un altro innesto dopo i 9-10 acquisti estivi.

La Juventus in questa situazione è pronta ad assecondare il suo allenatore, l'alternativa sarebbe quella di cambiare guida tecnica e non è questa oggi la strada che vuole seguire la società. Non facile però nella prossima finestra di calciomercato trovare un centravanti all'altezza della Juventus. Le alternative al momento sono principalmente due: o investire una quarantina di milioni per quel Varela che sta facendo sfracelli a Monza ma ha solo poche settimane in Serie A. Oppure - e questa è l'ipotesi più probabile - portare avanti la trattativa con l'Atletico Madrid per cedere Jonathan David a titolo definitivo ai colchoneros e far fare il viaggio opposto a quell'Alexander Sorloth che dall'alto dei suoi 196 centimetri il centravanti di lotta e di governo lo sa fare benissimo.

La bellissima vittoria contro il Frosinone dovrebbe far riflettere su cosa serva al Milan in vista del mercato di gennaio. I don't think the "Whoooaaaaaaa, dude!" award counts as a Game of the Year nod

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Valve has opened nominations for the 2026 Steam awards, and for this 10th iteration of the annual event, players are being asked to choose a special winner. The Hall of Fame award will go to one of the ten previous Steam Game of the Year award winners, but there's one problem: Steam didn't have a GOTY category for the first two years of these awards.

That means that the "winners" in 2016 and 2017, Grand Theft Auto 5 and The Witcher 3, were never actually voted on by fans as GOTY-caliber stars. Certainly, both of those games were widely acclaimed when they launched – but The Witcher 3 and the PC version of GTA 5 were both released in 2015, before the Steam awards even existed.

Going back to the first round, the Steam awards offered an array of goofball categories with silly names that nonetheless honored great games. In 2016, GTA 5 did win an award titled "Whoooaaaaaaa, dude!" for games that "melt your face," and in 2017, The Witcher 3 won the "Choices Matter" award for games with a great deal of player agency.

As Valve explains in an FAQ alongside today's nomination announcement, the team used these two awards as a "proxy" to decide on the 2016 and 2017 GOTY picks. But I've gotta ask, why did 2017 not just use "Whoooaaaaaaa, Dude! 2.0," won by The Evil Within 2, for its GOTY? Or the "Even Better Than I Expected" award, explicitly built for games released in 2017 and won by Cuphead?

In fairness, it is fun seeing all-time heavy hitters like GTA 5 and Witcher 3 going up against similar heavyweights like Elden Ring, Baldur's Gate 3, and Hollow Knight: Silksong. And ultimately, none of this matters – as with any set of game awards, it's supposed to simply be an opportunity to celebrate excellent games. But, when did we ever let an award show go by without using it instead as a chance to get mad about the nominees?

Get your appetite for award season going with all the Golden Joystick Awards 2026 winners.

His love for games was sparked somewhere between Metal Gear Solid 2 and Knights of the Old Republic, and these days you can usually find him splitting his entertainment time between retro gaming, the latest big action-adventure title, or a long haul in American Truck Simulator.

Check Who’s Posting Before You Trust Reddit On AEO Tools via @sejournal, @MattGSouthern

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Identify practical ways to improve location visibility in AI-generated answers and recommendations.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Learn how to prioritize page fixes, check agent access and measure readiness for your 2027 SEO plan.

Where search leaders are putting 2027 budget, plus the AI visibility scorecard that replaces session reporting.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

Webinar: State Of Agentic AEO For 2027: What’s Scaling & What’s Stalling

An analysis of subreddits flagged most users weighing in on AEO tools as likely promoters. Among the rest, accuracy was the top feature complaint.

Graphite flagged 55% of the users who gave an opinion of an AEO tool across 54 subreddits this year as likely working for or promoting a marketing software product.

Among the users it didn’t flag, the accuracy of visibility scores drew more unfavorable opinions than any other product feature.

The growth agency picked subreddits where AEO comes up regularly and found 5,990 posts and 25,392 comments from January through September that mention a tool. OpenAI language models labeled the mentions, the opinions and the likely promoters.

The agency helps companies with AEO and SEO and says it doesn’t sell AEO tools or accept payment to be mentioned. Its CEO, Ethan Smith, is an investor in Peec AI, one of three tools tied for the highest score in the analysis.

Graphite flagged 1,298 of the 2,365 Reddit users who gave an opinion of an AEO tool or a marketing platform’s AEO features.

A user was flagged when the agency’s model put the chance at 50% or more that their posts promoted a marketing product they build, own, work for, sell or partner with. That includes SEO, AEO, sales and web analytics tools. An agency’s description of its own client work did not constitute promotion.

To test the model, the company manually inspected 43 of the users it had flagged. Ultimately, 12% of them disclosed being promoters, while 62% were found to have promoted a product but failed to identify themselves as such; the remaining ones were judged non-promoters. The write-up also says that some of the models’ labeling decisions could be incorrect.

Four types of likely promoter Reddit accounts were identified. Each type exhibited a pattern across several posts or comments.

One account was used to recommend the same lesser-known tracking service on eight posts and comments. In January this user claimed that they switched from a competitor, and 11 days later they commented about possibly purchasing the product.

Another account made reference to a little-known product in four comments with well-known tracking services over the course of eight days.

A third account posted three separate times for the same product within 12 minutes.

Over six weeks, 38 accounts recommended the same product in 52 comments, each using the phrase “from what I’ve read” in its recommendation. That phrase appears with any other tool no more than twice.

Of the opinions regarding the accuracy of AEO tools from users who weren’t flagged, 143 were negative while 27 had positive views. No other feature elicited as many negative opinions. These users provided opinions on 44 tools, for a total of 1,557 opinions.

Graphite’s chart compares praise and criticism across features after excluding opinions from users it flagged as likely promoters.

According to the write-up, users frequently view visibility scores as estimates due to variation in AI answers among different runs, locations and users. Several have advised fellow users to “treat them as trend, not truth” and to verify the information through manual review.

The citation data, or the citations used by AI systems as source material for their answers, was generally viewed positively by users. Price received more user opinions than any other attribute and most of those opinions were negative, at 182 negative versus 98 positive.

The agency also evaluated the 11 tools which generated sufficient opinions using a 0-100 point scale; a score of 50 represented neutral. Each of the 11 tools scored between 54 and 66 points. The authors also said the ranges around many of the tools’ scores overlapped and therefore, the differences in scores could lack statistical significance.

Graphite’s count puts a number on how many Reddit users weighing in on AEO tools may be promoting marketing software. Before a tool recommended on Reddit goes on your shortlist, check the account’s history for repeat plugs and a story that changes from post to post.

Graphite’s tool scores reflect opinions from January through September, and the company says their order could change as more opinions come in. Complaints about the accuracy of visibility scores give you something to test before renewing. Run a sample of your tracked prompts by hand and compare the results with the tool’s numbers.

BepiColombo spacecraft becomes 2nd-ever mission to orbit Mercury — and it will soon split in two

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The BepiColombo spacecraft has overcome a last-minute computer glitch to enter orbit around Mercury — the 2nd craft in history to orbit the closest planet to the sun.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

After eight years slingshotting around the inner solar system, the joint European-Japanese spacecraft BepiColombo has finally entered into orbit around Mercury — becoming only the second mission ever to orbit the closest planet to the sun.

Mission scientists confirmed on Saturday (Nov. 21) that the spacecraft reached Mercury's orbit and is transmitting data back to the ground team.

However, the orbital insertion didn't go entirely as planned, European Space Agency (ESA) representatives said in a statement on Monday. Due to a software glitch, the spacecraft didn't fire its thrusters for the full 37 minutes required to slow its approach to Mercury.

While ESA investigates the issue and what it means for the spacecraft's trajectory, BepiColombo remains "safe," the statement noted. The spacecraft will hopefully be ready to begin a year of intensive science observations of Mercury starting a few months from now.

An illustration of BepiColombo approaching Mercury for orbital insertion.

Built jointly by ESA and the Japan Aerospace Exploration Agency (JAXA), BepiColombo is actually two separate probes, which are currently stacked one atop the other in orbit. The ESA-built orbiter, named the Mercury Planetary Orbiter (MPO), and the JAXA-built orbiter, the Mercury Magnetospheric Orbiter (Mio), carry separate science instruments, and they will soon diverge into separate orbital paths around the mysterious inner planet, operating as two probes gathering different streams of data.

A selfie of BepiColombo during a flyby of Mercury's north pole in 2025.

The pair of probes launched together aboard an Ariane 5 rocket in October 2018, and spent the following eight years on an interplanetary journey that took them past Earth, the moon and Venus. It even passed by Mercury itself six times. (During one of these flybys, in January 2025, Bepi snapped an incredible closeup photo of Mercury's north pole.)

This complicated cosmic choreography was necessary to prevent BepiColombo from picking up too much speed and overshooting Mercury's orbit, according to ESA. Mercury sits about 36 million miles (58 million kilometers) from our star, or roughly one third of the distance between Earth and the sun. That makes it very difficult for a spacecraft to reach Mercury without being captured by the sun's considerable gravitational pull and dragged off course.

Get the world’s most fascinating discoveries delivered straight to your inbox.

Only one other spacecraft — NASA's MESSENGER probe — has succeeded in entering Mercury orbit before, circling the planet from 2011 to 2015 after its own seven-year journey through space.

Assuming ESA determines that BepiColombo is fit to continue its mission as planned, then it will spend the next few months getting both probes in position to start mapping Mercury in the greatest detail ever.

MPO and Mio are expected to separate in early December, with Mio entering a highly elliptical orbit around Mercury's poles. MPO will then take a few more months to fine-tune its trajectory, reaching a lower and more circular polar orbit in March 2027, according to ESA. Its science operations are expected to begin in April.

The sibling probes will each orbit Mercury several times a day, with MPO completing an orbit every 2 hours or so. Observing the mysterious inner planet from several different vantage points, the probes will make the most comprehensive map of Mercury ever. The mission's suite of science instruments will study the planet across the electromagnetic spectrum, taking observations in X-rays, gamma-rays and ultraviolet light, as well as collecting detailed measurements of the planet's magnetic field.

With this information, scientists hope to unravel many mysteries of the least-explored planet in the solar system. The new data should enable scientists to tackle myriad long-standing questions: What is Mercury's interior made of, and where does its magnetic field come from? How was the planet formed, and to what extent is it still tectonically active today? By making precise measurements of the spacecraft's orbit and position, a radio experiment will also provide a new test of Einstein's theory of general relativity.

Stay tuned for more updates from ESA about humanity's first visit to Mercury in more than a decade.

He holds a bachelor's degree in creative writing from the University of Arizona, with minors in journalism and media arts. His interests include black holes, asteroids and comets, and the search for extraterrestrial life.

GTA 6 players found a '60 fps glitch', but it’s too unstable to stop me from wishing it was on PC already

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Spoiler: It doesn't actually run at 60 fps.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Clever Grand Theft Auto 6 players have found a glitch with the Xbox version of the game that allows players to forcibly remove Rockstar's 30 fps cap. But even though people claim the game jumps up to 60 fps, the actual performance is nowhere near as smooth, according to Digital Foundry's tests.

The glitch is surprisingly simple to perform: You just need to set the console's output to 60 Hz, load GTA 6, swap the output to 120 Hz, load a different game, and then use the Quick Resume feature to go back to GTA 6. For whatever reason, this forces the game to run above 30 fps, but not by much.

In Digital Foundry's testing, GTA 6 doesn't suddenly become a buttery smooth 60 fps dream. Instead, it has an average frame rate of about 36 fps and still drops below that number during particularly intense scenes, like in city areas or when multiple cars are exploding due to rocket launcher hijinks.

The most notable improvement is lower input lag when it manages to climb up to around 40 fps, but even that has an asterisk because the frame rate varies too much for the benefits to be consistent. Unless you plan on spending your time in Leonida roleplaying as a fisherman, you're going to have a bad time.

While Rockstar technology production director Marc Chambers said an official 60 fps mode for GTA 6 is too much for the current generation of consoles to handle, including the PS5 Pro, Digital Foundry theorizes that a 40 fps mode might be possible in the future.

I'm hopeful for console players, but none of this has stopped me from wanting the game on the only platform where 60 fps won't be much of a challenge to hit: PC. Tweaking settings you don't care as much about to juice the frame rate is much preferable over having to deal with a set "performance" mode or a glitch that could be patched out any day. A good PC version should be able to nicely scale up or down in performance based on your hardware setup, and Rockstar has shown that it can pull that off in the past with games like Red Dead Redemption 2.

For a game as good as GTA 6—Chris says it's "(mostly) great" in his 100-hour impressions— you shouldn't have to deal with quirks that tarnish the experience. I'm not a Rockstar employee, I don't know what challenges a PC version introduces or why it wasn't prioritized for a simultaneous PC and console release, but I do know that a PC version likely won't have as many caveats when it comes to performance as it does on consoles. So, I'll deal with Rockstar's intended 30 fps cap until the moment I can boot up GTA 6 on my PC, and then forget that I ever had to endure the limits of the current console experience.

U.S. intelligence chief says more tech outages are inevitably coming

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Over the past month, tech outages have disrupted work emails, delayed bank transactions, and temporarily grounded departing flights across the United States. Most of the biggest disruptions happened within six days of one another.

On Sunday, Director of National Intelligence Jay Clayton said Americans should expect more disruptions like these, but not because of an incoming cyberattack; rather, because companies and government agencies are now in the process of updating aging technology.

"We are going to have glitches, gaps, interruptions in service," Clayton told CNN's State of the Union on Sunday, Nov. 24. "That is going to happen." He said much of the country's digital infrastructure was built over three decades or more and needs upgrades to run better and stay secure. (As one example, the FAA is still in the process of phasing out floppy disks, with a target of retiring the '90s-era technology by 2028.)

Clayton compared it to weekend maintenance at work: A company might take a system offline on Saturday and plan to have it back by 2 A.M. Sunday. If the team finds another problem during the upgrade, it might be Sunday afternoon before the system is back up. "We are taking more systems down and putting them back up than ever before," he said.

It was a lot to keep track of in six days: On Nov. 12, some Microsoft Outlook users couldn't open their inboxes or send email. A day later, the Federal Aviation Administration paused departures nationwide after air traffic control communications problems. Then, on Nov. 16, a software bug delayed Chase transactions, and a separate outage briefly paused United Airlines departures. The following day, several Apple services ran into problems, and the National Security Agency's classified internal network went offline.

As Mashable has reported, despite the odd timing, the outages were not related, and Clayton told ABC's This Week that they were neither coordinated nor related to state-sponsored cyberattacks either. He attributed the disruptions to system upgrades he called "absolutely necessary going forward." "And we’re going to see more of that," he said. The best tech deal this week The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile Products available for purchase through affiliate links. If you buy something through links on our site, Mashable may earn an affiliate commission.

The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile

He also said, "The need to improve our digital infrastructure is clear."

AI is identifying zero-day bugs in software faster than ever, and fixing those flaws safely takes time. Some zero-day bug bounty programs have been forced to shut down entirely, and earlier this year, the Zero Day Initiative reported bug reports had increased 490 percent year over year. Companies now have to move quickly to patch all the new bugs found by AI.

Microsoft President Brad Smith called the pressure to move faster part of the "AI ripple effects" on CBS's Face the Nation on Nov. 22 — companies want to fix flaws before attackers can use them. On older systems, Smith said, moving that quickly can leave engineers with less time to test what happens when a fix goes live.

Dmitri Alperovitch, chair of the Silverado Policy Accelerator, described what that looks like for the people doing the work. "I.T. teams, network teams are overwhelmed. They're working 24/7. They're making mistakes," he said on the same program. He expects more outages as teams work through the growing number of flaws they've found.

The National Security Agency's outage offers one example of how an urgent fix can disrupt service. The agency's classified internal network went offline on Nov. 17 during work after the agency installed a security fix for PHP, a widely used programming language, Clayton told Fox Business. Those same flaws, known as P2PSHELL, had already forced some website operators to take their sites offline in October while they installed the fix, as Mashable reported at the time. OpenAI said that its GPT-5.6 Sol model had found the bugs.

Microsoft, on the other hand, traced its outage to a software bug introduced in July that went unnoticed for months. It only became a problem in November, when Microsoft sent more Teams traffic to servers that also handle Outlook email. Some of those servers were already offline, and the extra traffic triggered the bug on others. When those servers stopped responding, Microsoft automatically sent traffic to the ones that were still working. They started to fail, too.

Smith said the broader work could take months. His advice? Everyone should install security updates as soon they arrive on a phone or laptop.
