Why Digital Realty Trust Stock Moved Over 3% Higher on Monday

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Nvidia Effect was strong that trading session.

By Eric Volkman – Nov 23, 2026 at 5:46PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Digital Realty Trust ›

Investors were more than willing to put their trust in Digital Realty Trust (DLR +3.05%) stock at the start of the trading week. Shares of the data center real estate investment trust (REIT) ended Monday more than 3% higher, on the back of developments with one of the company's most important collaborators.

The No. 1 news item by far in the tech industry -- and indeed, global business -- was the reinstatement of Nvidia's (NVDA +31.48%) Jensen Huang as CEO. The move by that company's board of directors came mere days after Huang resigned his position following his admissions of extramarital affairs, one of which may have been with a person with ties to Chinese intelligence.

This, of course, doesn't impact Digital Realty directly. However, as a major data center operator, the REIT works closely with the world's leading artificial intelligence (AI) chipmaker in various ways. This includes work on hardware design, implementation, and research.

When an influential company changes its leadership -- as Nvidia did temporarily after Huang's abdication -- it can generate fear among investors that its business strategy will change. They worry that a new chief executive or management change will seek better, cheaper, or otherwise more advantageous ways of working with its key partners. With Huang's reinstatement, those fears have vanished -- at least for now.

Should we assume it'll be smooth sailing with Digital Realty and Nvidia from now on?

I think it would be risky to think so, although it's far more likely at this point that Huang stays than departs again. With many tech stocks seeing a lift on Nvidia's Monday news, it's important to distinguish those with good fundamentals and truly strong, durable ties to the chip giant. Those are the companies with potential for sustained gains. Digital Realty, which has lately posted record bookings, is one of them.

Nightmare at Dens Park: Dundee come to terms with history as child asks ‘what about last night?’

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

It wasn’t quite the morning after the night before. Nevertheless, Dundee players were very obviously still bearing the mental scars on Monday nearly 48 hours after a night of infamy inflicted by Hearts.

An old adage is that it’s best to go again as quickly as possible to get the performance and - most urgently of all – the result out of the system.

Did you know with a Digital Subscription to The Scotsman, you can get unlimited access to the website including our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

“To a point, yes…” agreed Steven Pressley when this theory was put to him at the sun-sprayed Dundee training facility at Gardyne College Campus ahead of Tuesday’s return to action against Hibs.

The counter argument, one the Dundee manager was clearly conscious of, is that three nights later is maybe just a bit too soon. In addition, when it comes to grounds Dundee might choose to visit for their next assignment after losing 8-0 – or should that be 0-8 – then Easter Road would not be high on the list. Dundee have not won there for a quarter of a century. The record books are being well-thumbed just now.

“We obviously don’t have a lot of time to work on elements of our game plan,” said Pressley. “It has been pretty frantic.

“Not only have we had to plan how we have to play and be prepared for Hibernian, I have also had to think a lot about the management of the group and everybody within that. I have had to find a way to stabilise the group.”

The employment of such words as “stabilise” reflect the harrowing nature of the experience. A group of 16 players including substitutes, many of them in their early 20s, endured something akin to a medical procedure.

A rampant Hearts side removed their sense of self-belief with almost surgical precision. Professional sport is often about how quickly such wounds can heal.

But this is new territory for everyone, including those as steeped in the game as Pressley. Owen Goodman, the 22-year-old keeper on loan from Crystal Palace, was perhaps left with most to feel sore about on Saturday night as goal after goal whizzed past him.

Dundee presumably have a duty of care towards him. It’s maybe one reason why Pressley decided against showing a re-run of the match the following morning. “I didn’t think putting them through the agony of watching it again was helpful,” he said.

Goodman was the last Dundee player to leave the field and one of the first in on Monday. He mentioned never having let in eight goals before, even in academy games. This was new territory for so many.

Young men such as Goodman carry a huge burden of responsibility as they attempt to bounce back from a historic defeat. An updated edition of the definitive Dundee FC history book, They Wore the Dark Blue, has just been published. It’s already out of date in one respect. Although it contains every result, competitive and otherwise, dating back to 1893, the Dundee fans' bible doesn’t include what now stands as the club’s biggest-ever home loss.

Such landmark results usually belong in the late 1800s, rendered a curiosity rather than a wounding detail due to the passage of time.

In Dundee’s case, prior to Saturday, their biggest-ever home defeat was relatively recent – 1971, against a Celtic side who had been crowned European champions in recent enough memory for one newspaper to describe them having “conjured the magic of Lisbon” at Dens Park following their 8-1 win.

Aberdeen equalled the margin of victory if not the scoreline in 2017. But Hearts outdid both those results on Saturday night. When Stuart Findlay rose like Alan Gilzean to nod in No 8 with a minute or so of the game left, the old pillars in the Dens Park main stand might have warped a fraction in recognition of the goal’s significance. Even if few, least of all Findlay, were probably aware of it at the time. A historic low dating back to 1893 – 1899 in the case of Dens Park itself – had been recorded.

Extraordinary, unthinkable results do happen. Just ask Hearts. Five goals down to Falkirk at home just a few weeks ago, they at least rallied to 5-2. Rallying was something Dundee conspicuously failed to do, which might have been the most disappointing aspect of the night for Pressley personally. He shouldered the blame himself, citing his suspicion that a poor performance had been in the post. He just didn't realise how poor. When he sought to rectify things at half time by sending on three substitutes there was precious little reaction. If anything things got worse, with Dundee reduced to ten men due to Owen Bevan’s red card.

Often teams who rack up handsome half-time leads cannot maintain such intensity in the second half due to a combination of factors, including stiff resolve from the opposition to avoid further embarrassment. This wasn’t evident on Saturday. Hearts scored another four goals to match their first-half quota and might have scored more, with another chalked off for a handball.

Although he says he did not consider tendering his resignation, it’s safe to assume Pressley endured a long dark night of the soul on Saturday night when he returned home to Edinburgh.

He said he found strength “from I don’t know where” and has been buoyed from a raft of messages of support. Perhaps Drey Wright, the Dundee wing-back, got to the heart of what it all meant on a human level.

“My six-year-old was at home watching the game, getting very upset with every goal that's going in,” said the 30-year-old.

“My eight-year-old is putting his hand on the shoulder of my wife, kind of subconsciously helping her out. So that's not easy to hear.

“I was back in training yesterday and later I was at the dinner table and my eldest says: ‘So Dad, what about that score last night?’ And it’s this kind of a back-to-reality moment. There’s no getting away from it.”

Tarkowski's first Premier League red ruins Moyes' night - was it deserved?

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Janelt scores stunner as Brentford beat 10-man Everton

It had been some run for Everton captain James Tarkowski.

Prior to Monday night, the defender had received 74 yellow cards in the Premier League but had never been sent off - a top-flight record.

But that run came to an end at Brentford as he was given his first red card in his 351st Premier League appearance, although the decision has attracted plenty of debate.

His dismissal crucially means he will miss Sunday's Merseyside derby and also ruined David Moyes' record-breaking night as he took charge of his 593rd game as Everton boss - more than anyone else in the club's history.

The 4-0 loss was also the heaviest defeat Everton have had since Moyes returned to the club at the start of last year.

Tarkowski's dismissal proved a big talking point during and after the match, with opinions split on whether he should have gone. Even Moyes wasn't sure.

He told BBC Match of the Day: "My thoughts at the time, I didn't think it was but when I've looked at it again I think his foot high. Is there enough force for it to be a red card? Not sure about that."

James Tarkowski received his first Premier League red card

Just 15 minutes after Keane Lewis-Potter scored his second deflected strike of the evening, Everton skipper Tarkowski thundered into a challenge on Brentford's Yehor Yarmoliuk.

He won the ball but also followed through with the challenge forcefully. Initially, referee Craig Pawson deemed a foul and a yellow card was enough.

But, after an intervention from the video assistant referee (VAR) and studying the replay, Pawson changed the yellow for a red.

The Premier League match centre said on X: "After VAR review, the referee overturned the original decision of yellow card for Tarkowski for the challenge on Yarmoliuk and issued a red card for serious foul play.

"VAR: 'Point of contact is on the shin. He's got speed and intensity. Studs are high.'

"REF: 'After review, Everton No.6 commits a challenge that endangers his opponent's safety, making contact with the studs onto the shin. Cancelling the yellow card - and a red card.'"

Referees are asked to take into account several considerations for what counts as serious foul play including the speed of the challenge, its intensity, force and what degree of control there was.

But, as always, there was plenty of debate about whether it was the correct decision.

BBC Sport's football issues correspondent Dale Johnson, said: "Really not sure about this as a VAR red card.

"Yes, Tarkowski absolutely ticks the boxes for speed and intensity and leading high with studs showing.

"But the question marks are that Tarkowski made only small contact and didn't have a straight leg when he did so.

"Maybe his reputation played a part? Remember, he wrongly escaped a VAR red card against Liverpool for a challenge, which was along the same lines. He won the ball and then followed through into Alexis Mac Allister. Thought that was a far stronger challenge."

BBC Radio 5 Live's - and former Blackburn Rovers striker - Chris Sutton, said on X: "Come on that's not a redâ¦ the game's gone."

Former Arsenal striker Alan Smith agreed the decision was harsh. He told Sky Sports: "Ten years ago we wouldn't have looked at it once and now we still [frame] it on the follow-through. I just can't see it.

"Only Tarkowski knows if he wanted to leave it high or if it was natural. The follow-through was just natural momentum.

"There's a bit of anger in there from Tarkowski and it's a little high but only he knows if he wanted to leave a little more on Yarmoliuk there."

Opinion was far from unanimous though, with both pundits in the Sky Sports studio adamant it was a clear red.

Former Liverpool centre-back Jamie Carragher said: "It's a red card. Tarkowski has got previous for tackles like that. He is a 90s defender, he tackles like that.

"He follows through with such force, it is excessive. You want to give him the benefit of the doubt but it's a red card."

He added: "You could argue was it a big enough mistake for VAR to get involved but I think we are splitting hairs. He doesn't need to do this."

While ex-Totenham defender Toby Alderweireld added: "It's a red card, he knows what he is doing. 100%."

Brentford 4-0 Everton: David Moyes post-match interview

Why CrowdStrike Stock Was Down Today

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

CrowdStrike beat Wall Street's estimates, but its $43.9 billion deal to buy Zscaler sent the stock lower.

By Johnny Rice – Nov 23, 2026 at 5:40PM EST

10 stocks we like better than CrowdStrike ›

CrowdStrike Holdings (CRWD -8.92%) stock finished down 8.9% on Monday, Nov. 23, after the cybersecurity company agreed to buy Zscaler (ZS +17.67%).

It was one of the few tech stocks to see red today, with the Nasdaq Composite up 6.2%. The S&P 500 jumped 4.7%.

CrowdStrike announced Monday that it would buy Zscaler, a cloud security company, in an all-stock deal valued at $43.9 billion. Zscaler shareholders would own about 16% of the combined company, and CrowdStrike shareholders would own the rest.

The deal still needs approval from both regulators and Zscaler shareholders.

The offer represents a 34% premium to Zscaler's Friday closing price; investors seemed to think that's a lot to pay.

CrowdStrike makes software that protects devices, while Zscaler helps companies control who can access their apps and data. Bringing the two together makes sense from a product standpoint and gives the company more to sell to its own customers as well as a shot at selling its software to roughly 8,000 Zscaler customers who don't already buy from CrowdStrike.

The announcement overshadowed a strong earnings report this morning in which the company beat Wall Street estimates for revenue and adjusted earnings per share. Sales jumped 28% year over year.

Tech Moves: AWS nabs Salesforce exec; former OpenAI employees launch Seattle startup; Portal hire

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Tech Moves covers notable hires, promotions and personnel changes in the Pacific NW tech community. Submissions: [email protected]

by Lisa Stiffler on Nov 23, 2026 at 2:39 pmNovember 23, 2026 at 2:43 pm

— Muralidhar Krishnaprasad has been named as vice president of analytics, storage and databases at Amazon Web Services, a role that reports to AWS CEO Matt Garman. Krishnaprasad, who goes by MK, left Salesforce after eight years. He was president and chief technology officer there, where he helped build the company’s agentic enterprise platform.

At AWS, he will lead an organization that unites Amazon Simple Storage Service (S3), database and analytics services.

“MK brings deep technical roots, a customer-obsessed mindset and experience building data platforms at global scale,” Garman said in an email to staff.

Earlier in his career, MK spent more than a decade each at Oracle and Microsoft.

— After more than 13 years at Microsoft, Bonnie McCracken has been named VP of strategy and chief technology officer for communications. She is also general manager of innovation and thought leadership communications.

— Skye Scofield, Pablo Beltran and Brent Echols left OpenAI roughly two months ago to launch a Seattle startup that is currently in stealth mode. Scofield served as chief of staff to OpenAI’s Vijaye Raji, CTO of applications. Beltran and Echols, both software engineers, were members of the company’s technical staff.

More recently, Brandon Zinn joined the stealth startup as head of sales. All four overlapped in earlier roles at Statsig. In a post today on LinkedIn, Zinn asked for leads with home services owners “who could use help getting more work or making the business easier to run.”

The team is working with business owners in “roofing, tree care, plumbing, HVAC, painting, etc. Especially teams of 3–15 people where a lot still falls directly on the owner,” Zinn said.

Beltran said in a separate post that the startup has passed $100,000 in annual recurring revenue and is rapidly developing its software.

— Portal Space Systems, a Bothell, Wash.-based space company, has appointed Egbert Maben as principal satellite guidance, navigation and control (GNC) engineer — the startup’s first dedicated GNC hire. Last month, Portal reached Earth orbit for the second time this year.

Maben joined from Blue Origin, where he was a leader in GNC and hardware integration and helped resolve mission-critical hardware failures. He also tackled assembly, integration and test, a process that verifies the performance of software and hardware components. He previously spent nearly two decades at Lockheed Martin.

Maben, the company notes, has been keeping bees since ninth grade. “The only thing that’s left to see is whether Egbert, like bees, will be using dance to communicate those directives to our spacecraft,” Portal said on LinkedIn.

— Seattle-based Yoodli has named Jessica Evans as its first mid-market sales leader.The startup recently expanded from helping people practice speeches, sales pitches and job interviews to providing corporate training across a wide range of skills. Evans joins from ClickUp.

— PATH, a Seattle-based global health nonprofit, has appointed three new board members:

Ladybird, the first new browser engine in years, is now available to test

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Looking for a web browser without Google Chrome underneath? You can try this independent web browser now on macOS and Linux.

Looking for a web browser without Google Chrome underneath? You can try this independent web browser now on macOS and Linux.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Ladybird, an independent web browser with a built-from-scratch engine, is finally available for testing. On Monday, Ladybird developer Andreas Kling announced that the browser is launching in alpha on macOS and Linux following more than four years of development.

The release makes Ladybird the first browser in years to arrive with an entirely new engine. We’ve seen many engines emerge over time, but the most popular browsers use just three today: Gecko, Blink, and WebKit. While Gecko, now used in Mozilla Firefox, first appeared in Netscape 6 in 2000, Apple’s WebKit arrived in Safari in 2003 as a fork of KDE’s KHTML. A decade later, the Google-backed Chromium project forked WebKit to create Blink, which now powers Google Chrome, Microsoft Edge, Opera, and more.

Ladybird is backed by a nonprofit organization with the goal of creating “an independent, fast and secure browser that respects user privacy and fosters an open web,” according to its website. Though Ladybird development initially began as part of a C++ project, its developers eventually began porting the code to Rust using AI.

In his post announcing the alpha, Kling notes that the browser built around the Ladybird engine “is modest but functional,” adding that “some websites won’t work” during the alpha. A Windows version of Ladybird will arrive during a “later stage” of the project.

Why SK Hynix Stock Skyrocketed Today

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The chipmaker rose after reports of interest from a high-stakes bidder.

By Danny Vena, CPA – Nov 23, 2026 at 5:36PM EST

Shares of SK Hynix (SKHY +20.00%) charged sharply higher on Wednesday, jumping as much as 23.7%. By the time the market closed, the stock was still up 20%.

The catalyst that sent the semiconductor specialist higher was news of a high-profile potential suitor.

SK Hynix was reportedly the subject of takeover interest from Japanese investment holding company and tech conglomerate SoftBank Group (SFTBY +19.27%). Chairman Masayoshi Son approached numerous Wall Street banks to discuss financing options to acquire the chipmaker for $1 trillion, according to a report that first appeared in The Information. The price under consideration would have been roughly 1.7 times SK Hynix's market cap of $576 billion as of Friday's market close.

The sheer magnitude of the offer and the potential national security implications were just a few of the obstacles that reportedly scuttled the deal, though it isn't clear whether a formal offer was ever tendered.

SoftBank has made no secret of its ambition to achieve end-to-end control over all elements of the artificial intelligence (AI) semiconductor supply chain. The company already owns data center chip designer Ampere Computing and power infrastructure specialist SB Energy, as well as an 86% controlling stake in chip designer Arm Holdings. SoftBank also holds a position in AI start-up OpenAI.

Bringing SK Hynix into the fold would have given the company a direct pipeline to DRAM, NAND flash, and high-bandwidth memory (HBM). However, in a statement, it said, "SoftBank explores many opportunities, and this one is no longer under active consideration." The wording suggests the deal was, in fact, explored.

So is SK Hynix worth $1 trillion? It's doubtful. A premium of that magnitude would likely have drawn the ire of smaller SoftBank investors, even as Son holds a dominant 33% stake in the company. Moreover, as Korea's dominant maker of HBM, it's unlikely the deal would have passed regulatory muster.

At less than 7 times earnings, SK Hynix stock appears attractively priced. However, the historical boom-and-bust cycle has some investors concerned, and the jury is still out on whether the company can maintain its juicy profit margins while the future of AI plays out.

Adam Driver: ‘No One Was Paying Us’ to Develop ‘The Hunt for Ben Solo’ and ‘Star Wars’ Boss Said ‘We Have a 100% Approval’ to Make It, Then Disney Killed It

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Adam Driver and Steven Soderbergh didn’t get paid for the two-and-a-half years they spent developing “The Hunt for Ben Solo,” a standalone “Star Wars” movie that would’ve centered on Driver’s fan favorite character Kylo Ren/Ben Solo. The actor said on the “Talk Easy” podcast that money was not even top of mind since they were given the upmost assurance from then-Lucasfilm president Kathleen Kennedy that the movie would happen, and thus the pay of it all could be figured out once it was greenlit. Related Stories Jesse Eisenberg and Julianne Moore on ‘The Debut,’ Testing AI to ‘Make Sure It’s Bad’ and Hoping ‘Now You See Me 4’ Gets Made Oscar Predictions: Best Actress — How Obsessed Will the Academy Become With Inde Navarrette?

“We were gonna do a Ben Solo movie and that didn’t happen. And we worked on it for a long time,” Driver said on the podcast. “Soderbergh did that for nothing, because we were given a lot of confidence. Like Kathy [Kennedy] said we have a 100% approval rate [from Disney]. We worked on it. No one’s paying us to work on it. The only reason we were working on it was we fucking loved the idea, how we wanted to do it, and I loved that character. And that was it. That was our guiding principle. All the money stuff came later. We were trying to figure out how to make it cheap so they could let us do what we wanted to do. So that was kind of a bummer.”

Driver revealed the existence of “The Hunt for Ben Solo” in an October 2025 interview with The Associated Press, explaining how Disney executives pulled the plug on the project because they couldn’t wrap their heads around the character coming back after he presumably died in 2019’s “Star Wars: The Rise of Skywalker.”

“I always was interested in doing another ‘Star Wars,’” Driver said at the time. “I had been talking about doing another one since 2021. Kathleen [Kennedy] had reached out. I always said: ‘With a great director and a great story, I’d be there in a second.’ I loved that character and loved playing him.”

In an AP interview earlier this month, Driver said it was “criminal” for Disney to scrap “The Hunt for Ben Solo” after also saying on “The Rich Eisen Show” around the same time that the project is officially dead and he has no interest in trying to revive it (“I think the ship has sailed though,” Driver said).

“For me it was: Steven Soderbergh wants to make a ‘Star Wars’ film. What are we debating?” Driver told AP. “It’s what it is. It’s criminal. Or it felt criminal. We’re all moving on. On to the next. And Steven already has something else that’s one of the best things he’s ever written.”

Driver originally told the AP in 2025 that he loved “The Hunt for Ben Solo” script, calling it the “standard” of what a “Star Wars” movie “should be” and comparing it to the “handmade and character-driven” feel of “The Empire Strikes Back.” He added that Lucasfilm execs Kennedy, Dave Filoni and Cary Beck backed the project until Disney shut it down.

“We presented the script to Lucasfilm. They loved the idea,” Driver recalled. “They totally understood our angle and why we were doing it. We took it to Bob Iger and Alan Bergman, and they said no. They didn’t see how Ben Solo was alive. And that was that.”

Kathleen Kennedy expressed her own frustration with Disney pulling the plug on the project during her Lucasfilm exit interview. Soderbergh told BK Mag soon after that he was not surprised to read about Kennedy’s annoyances.

“We were all frustrated,” Soderbergh said. “You know, that was two and a half years of free work for me and Adam and [writer] Rebecca Blunt. When Adam and I discussed him talking about it publicly, I said, ‘Look, do not editorialize or speculate about the why. Just say what happened, because all we know is what happened.’ The stated reason was, ‘We don’t think Ben Solo could be alive.’ And that was all we were told. And so there’s nothing to do about it, you know, except move on.”

“I’d kind of made the movie in my head, and just felt bad that nobody else was going to get to see it,” the director continued. “I thought the conversation was strictly going to be a practical one—where they go, what is this going to cost? And I had a really good answer for that. But it never even got to that point. It’s insane. We’re all very disappointed.”

Watch Driver’s full interview on the “Talk Easy” podcast in the video below.

US adds Megaspeed, at center of Nvidia scandal, to a trade blacklist

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Trump administration has added Megaspeed to a trade blacklist after tensions over its failure to do so raised questions. The news came the same day that Jensen Huang was reinstated as Nvidia's CEO.

The Trump administration added Megaspeed International, the firm at the heart of the Nvidia scandal, to a trade blacklist on Monday.

That means that the Singapore-based firm will not be able to obtain US technology, including semiconductors, without obtaining a license from the US government.

The news was reported by Semafor, an American news website.

The US Commerce Department had been investigating whether Megaspeed was smuggling Nvidia chips into China for more than a year, according to the Financial Times.

But tensions at the Commerce Department broke into the spotlight after Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang admitted to an extramarital affair with an executive at the Singapore-based firm earlier this month.

Politicians criticized Commerce Department Secretary Howard Lutnick for not having previously added Megaspeed to the department's so-called "Entity List" despite US intelligence warnings that the company was a possible front for smuggling sensitive AI chip technology to China.

Huang briefly lost his job over the scandal as US officials said the woman, Alice Huang, was a Chinese spy.

Jensen Huang was reinstated as CEO on Monday, in a stunning reversal after his resignation just two weeks ago.

The New York Times reported last month that US authorities had concluded that Alice Huang was a Chinese intelligence asset who passed information to Beijing while developing a close relationship with Jensen Huang.

Beijing has denied those claims and called the spy accusations "groundless."

Nvidia shares soared more than 30% after the company announced it reinstated Jensen Huang as CEO.

Some people were happy to see him back as president of Nvidia, as videos posted to social media show.

House Democratic leader Hakeem Jeffries, who is on track to be the next speaker of the House, called the decision to reinstate Huang "indefensible, irresponsible, and incomprehensible" and vowed Congress would reinvestigate any wrongdoing by the company and the Commerce Department.

12 Gifts to Level Up Your Next Family Adventure

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

If you buy through our links, we may earn an affiliate commission. This supports our mission to get more people active and outside.Learn about Outside Online's affiliate link policy

Your kids might not remember what was under the tree, but we promise they’ll never forget the nights spent under the stars with their family. Lucky for parents, outdoor gear is more family-friendly than ever. Think: tents you can stand up in, paddle boards that pack into a backpack, campsite games, passenger-friendly e-bikes, and so much more. These 12 adventure-ready gifts are for the families on your list who’d rather spend the weekend at the campsite, on the water, or on the bike path than on the couch with an iPad in hand.

The best part of birding is that there’s no real age requirement or limit. It’s arguably one of the most accessible (and relaxing) ways for families to enjoy the outdoors. Everyone from toddlers to grandparents can find joy in spotting bird species with these lightweight binoculars. Heck, you can get the grandparents out there, too! Psst…we have lots more birding gear recs where this came from.

You don’t have to give up hiking if you’ve got little ones. It just takes some helpful gear to keep the adventure going strong. Add a hiking backpack carrier to your gear closet so it’s easy to get your kids on the trail. Backpack carriers are best for youngins under 5 years old or 55 pounds. Our favorite pick for longer hiking trails is the Deuter Kid Comfort Child Carrier, which fits like a backpacking pack and includes tons of features to make hiking with kids much easier. And if you’re lucky, you just might squeeze some trail naps in.

Camp games, anyone? ENO’s new TrailFlyer game is like bags (or cornhole, as we call it in the Midwest) but designed for camping. It packs down into a small carrying case and takes just a few minutes to set up. Wrap the adjustable straps around a tree trunk or sturdy anchor point, stake out the feet, and start tossing.

Our family has put the heavy-duty Victory Patio Blanket to use as a play area for our crawling baby at camp, and we’ve taken it to plenty of beach days and park picnics. The underside is coated in a thick, waterproof material that keeps moisture and dirt from seeping through. You can also toss it in your tent to make a cozier floor or dress up your campsite with an outdoor rug.

Good luck wrapping a kayak (or fitting it in your garage). Skip the bulky gift for the packable and portable inflatable ISLE Switch 3. This hybrid SUP-kayak fits in a backpack with everything you need to hit the water, including a paddle, pump, and inflatable kayak seats. It’s the perfect adventure gift for a family who loves getting out on the water.

Keep it old school with this point-and-shoot camera that’s perfectly nostalgic. Don’t let its lack of a screen fool you. This screen-free digital camera is rechargeable and can capture up to 500 photos per charge. It also offers several built-in filters—standard, vintage, analog, and black-and-white—that you can swap with the push of a button. Bonus: it comes with a microSD card already inside, so you’ve got room for around 2,000 photos from day one.

If you want to take the littlest adventures with all their gear along, this wagon makes it easy. It works three ways: as a wagon for hauling, as a stroller with two 5-point safety harness seats, and as a car seat carrier with the included adapter (car seat not included).

The Wrangler Deluxe holds up to 110 pounds and brings the tough, trail-ready spirit Jeep is known for, with all-terrain puncture-proof airless tires and swivel front wheels for easy maneuvering. A removable cooler bag holds up to 16 cans, and the wagon also has a UV-blocking canopy, pull-down shades, a cup holder, a parent organizer, and multiple storage pockets. When it’s time to pack up, it folds compactly.

Cozy up around the campfire with the ones you love in this two-seater camp chair. Its low-profile, reclined design puts you at just the right height for roasting s’mores and soaking up the fire’s warmth. And for a chair that fits two (or three if you’ve got a kiddo on your lap), it packs down surprisingly small.

We can’t mention s’mores without plugging our favorite roasting sticks. These reusable, telescoping metal roasting forks make great stocking stuffers. Our set has lasted through years of camping, and we love using them not only for marshmallows but also for hot dogs, sausages, and the occasional Twinkie.

Due for a new family cooler? Keep beers, bubbly water, juice boxes, and everything in between ice-cold in the Hydro Flask 15L Carryout Soft Cooler. It’s easy to carry and easy to clean, which makes it a go-to for camping trips, road trips, soccer tournaments, and backyard grill-outs. Don’t worry about scratches, broken buckles, or dings, either: Hydro Flask offers a lifetime warranty.

Who needs a minivan when you’ve got the Aventon Abound LR? This cargo e-bike is made for family adventures in the city and beyond, featuring an extra-long rear rack that fits two kids (or one kiddo plus panniers full of gear). Hills are no match for its powerful 750W motor, and you can ride up to 60 miles on a single charge. We opted for the smaller Abound SR since we’re a “one-and-done” family, but the LR is a great upgrade if you have more kids and gear to haul.

Give the gift of camping! Hipcamp takes the stress out of snagging a campsite before it sells out. Its database of private land available for rent connects you with one-of-a-kind stays—from tent sites on farms and vineyards to cabins, glamping tents, and treehouses. All you have to do is dream up your family’s perfect night under the stars.

The perfect family camping tent doesn’t exis—oh wait, there’s The North Face Wawona 6. It’s tall enough to stand up in, sleeps six, features tons of interior pockets, and has color-coded poles to make setup a breeze. Speaking of a breeze, oversized mesh windows help ventilate the tent on muggy summer nights. The best part? A huge vestibule that works like a front porch, giving you a shady and dry spot to kick back in camp chairs in hot or rainy weather.

Nvidia Is Now Too Big to Hold to Account

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

This issue is preventing our website from loading properly. Please review the following troubleshooting tips or contact us at [email protected].

Argument: Nvidia Is Now Too Big to Hold to Account

Create an FP account to save articles to read later.

Downloadable PDFs are a benefit of an FP subscription.

Contact us at [email protected] to learn about upgrade options, unlocking the ability to gift this article.

Get audio access with any FP subscription.

Put out of mind the scenes of cheering you may have seen of Jensen Huang’s return to the headquarters of perhaps the world’s most important company, Nvidia, marking his personally triumphant comeback from being unanimously ousted by his board.

Get out of your thoughts any happy notions that you have reason to celebrate just because your money, like the money of so many millions of Americans and people well beyond the country’s borders, is tied up in the U.S. stock market, which soared at this surprising turn in the news after slumping badly in response to the scandals that had swirled around Huang.

Put out of mind the scenes of cheering you may have seen of Jensen Huang’s return to the headquarters of perhaps the world’s most important company, Nvidia, marking his personally triumphant comeback from being unanimously ousted by his board.

Get out of your thoughts any happy notions that you have reason to celebrate just because your money, like the money of so many millions of Americans and people well beyond the country’s borders, is tied up in the U.S. stock market, which soared at this surprising turn in the news after slumping badly in response to the scandals that had swirled around Huang.

What has happened in recent days, culminating in Huang’s return in the face of allegations he sustained a secret affair with a woman who was in touch with China’s spy agencies, is bad news for an economy that has grown increasingly imbalanced with the enormous growth of artificial intelligence. Far better for the U.S. economy and for everyone who has a stake in it would have been a reckoning for Nvidia that forced it to undertake the hard work of corporate reform and rebuilding trust.

Nvidia, for those who have not been paying attention, makes the most indispensable product in the world of AI: graphics processing units, a specialized type of chip, so capable and advanced that it has few real competitors, including, crucially, among well-funded Chinese companies. Nvidia, however, is much more than even this. As I have written before, it sits at the center of a highly incestuous economy of AI companies, including most of the big service providers, which it funds to keep purchasing its chips. The circular funding arrangements that Nvidia sustains with its largest customers—companies with extraordinary financial leverage and seemingly bottomless capital needs—is what keeps the AI economy’s party going. Without this, the merry-go-round of this new sector would grind to a halt and make what many thought looked like a bursting bubble in recent days seem like a minor blip.

Add to this the fact that the U.S. government and President Donald Trump have seemingly become convinced that everything and anything must be permitted with Nvidia, and with AI in general, or else China will overtake the United States in the most important technology of the near future. What this gives you is something more frightening than too big to fail: a company declared too strategically important to question or to hold to account.

The reality is that almost everything about the present picture is messed up and deeply worrisome. Let’s start with the fact that Trump, who already leads a government largely unconstrained by Congress and with wildly inflated executive powers, has taken so freely to intervening in the running of major companies. In Huang’s case, he played a central role in his reinstatement, as Monday’s news revealed.

There is nothing reassuring about this, but it gets worse. There is no reason to believe that Trump has an even remotely sophisticated grasp of the technologies at the heart of Nvidia’s business or its race against competitors. What we do know is that Trump has a weakness for very powerful and wealthy men, and Huang fits this billing. In the president’s universe, he is a winner, and Trump loves to associate himself with anyone invested with such an aura. Trump even posted an AI-generated photo of Huang intended to mirror a famous image of himself after surviving an assassination attempt, with the big difference that Huang was shown hoisting a red bra.

Trump, furthermore, is not known for a deep concern about ethics, and that is putting things gently. Where do ethics enter into this? Everywhere, from Huang’s honesty and the extent to which he was forthcoming about his affair with the suspicious Chinese national and shadowy customer, Alice Huang, as well as with at least one other woman who was his direct subordinate at Nvidia, in violation of corporate rules. Ethics are also involved in the way Huang ran his company, with a historically supine board, minimal internal controls on his behavior and oversight, and the way he promoted his family members. What matters is that nothing, inside the company or outside it, checked him. In fact, it seems likely that Trump, with his own record of serial marriages, rumored affairs, and children on the make, can relate.

But even these are mere starters. If competition with China deserves inclusion in any serious conversation about the future of AI, Trump’s treatment of Huang’s situation at Nvidia has been a running chronicle of flagrant disregard for national security. Let’s count the ways. First, when the U.S. government began to look into Alice Huang’s behavior on the suspicion that she might be a spy, it never informed Jensen Huang or gave him what would have been typical: a so-called defensive briefing so that he would know what to look out for and avoid giving away valuable trade secrets or selling his company’s most advanced chips without adequate controls preventing them from being shipped onward to China.

More broadly, historians may come to see Trump’s casual disregard for normal considerations of national security to be one of the most important and unusual hallmarks of his time in power. The examples are numerous. When shown evidence that Russia or China or some other powerful adversary is conducting secret operations against U.S. interests, Trump has often waved away concerns—in effect saying, well, we do that to them, too.

Last week offered the spectacle of Trump mugging in China with that country’s leader and Russia’s Vladimir Putin, with no hint that he had complained to them about offering targeting information to Iran that has enabled that country to attack U.S. bases in the Middle East, killing U.S. troops, as is the case with Russia, or supplying drone components to Iran, as with China. Trump went yet further, though, looking like a kid in a candy shop as he entered a room to greet an awaiting Putin and exclaiming, “He’s my friend.” Lest the message be lost on anyone, he later posted a picture of the meeting with this same message on social media.

Second, there is further reason to distrust the judgment of the U.S. government in the unfolding Nvidia saga. It lies in the vague and unforthcoming way its officials have spoken about Huang and his erstwhile Chinese mistress since their affair became public knowledge, always carefully avoiding clarity about whether or not she might have been a spy or whether his behavior damaged U.S. national security interests.

In fairness, the question of whether she was a spy involves some nuance. Alice Huang could very well have compromised U.S. national security without being a member or agent of any Chinese intelligence service in a formal sense. The lack of openness and straight language about the other question leaves little wiggle room, though. Through the Singapore-based company she worked for, Alice Huang was purchasing Nvidia’s most advanced chips, which fall under explicit U.S. export controls to China. This puts the public in a bewildering place. On the one hand, people are asked to believe that the United States must beat China in a zero-sum race toward the AI future. On the other, given the lack of candor about these Nvidia sales and the damage they may have caused to U.S. interests, we are being told, in effect, one of Trump’s favorite messages: Smile, and be happy. Lots of money was being made.

Taken together, Nvidia and Jensen Huang seem to concentrate enormous and growing risk for the entire public, as does the broader AI sector. Remember that Anthropic, the leading AI provider that went public recently, has warned of the potential power of its systems becoming so awesome as to having the potential to lead us all to an extinction-level event. And when the world’s top AI power is led by someone with such a casual disregard for safeguards, for strong governance, for ethics and for transparency, a company too important to question invites a peril that risks falling on us all.

Howard W. French is a columnist at Foreign Policy, a professor at the Columbia University Graduate School of Journalism, and a longtime foreign correspondent. His latest book is The Second Emancipation: Nkrumah, Pan-Africanism, and Global Blackness at High Tide. Bluesky: @hofrench.bsky.social X: @hofrench

Duolingo's 2026 Outlook: AI Personalization Sustains Growth for 65 Million Daily Users

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

This under-the-radar software stock boasts a Superscore of 89 from our Hidden Gems Primary database, part of The Motley Fool's Moneyball Database system. Here's why.

By Anders Bylund – Updated Nov 23, 2026 at 5:33PM EST

You sit down with a mobile app to learn a new language, but you soon realize you are not just learning vocabulary. You are part of an elaborate, data-hungry experiment that spans 65 million daily active users. Duolingo (DUOL +1.43%), a Pittsburgh-based company, has turned language education into a high-engagement, gamified machine. The stock currently trades at $152.31 as of Nov. 19, 2026, though it has experienced a volatile year, with a 15% decline over the last 12 months as of Nov. 23.

Our proprietary Hidden Gems scoring system assigns Duolingo an overall Superscore of 89 out of 100, placing it in the Strong category. This score ranks it in the Top ~1% of all companies we analyze, ahead of roughly 99 out of every 100 stocks in our database. The Superscore serves as one data-driven signal for your research; this article balances the operational strengths that drive that number with the risks that keep it from being higher so you can weigh the thesis for yourself.

Because the business is highly capital-efficient, it earns substantial profit on a relatively small base of tangible assets, which is a rare quality that allows it to turn each percentage point of revenue growth into outsize returns for shareholders. Investors are often willing to pay a higher multiple for this kind of efficiency, though it does not eliminate the valuation risk inherent in the current share price.

You may want to keep researching before buying if...

The Superscore is just one data-driven signal worth investigating, not a stand-alone recommendation, and you should always weigh these findings against your personal risk tolerance and financial goals before deciding to act.

Duolingo has been a difficult stock to love lately. It's down 73% from the all-time peak in May 2025, haunted by slowing user growth and unstable profit margins. Skeptics worry about generative AI stealing Duolingo's lunch money, as chatbots look ready to teach anyone anything, all in a phone app or a browser window.

I think that's a mistake, based on my experience in several language-oriented businesses.

As long as these conditions hold, Duolingo will have a place in the online learning market. The lessons learned from language-learning courses have already yielded a hit in the popular chess curriculum, with 10 million daily active users and a solid setup for future in-app purchases.

And with the help of AI back-end tools, Duolingo plans to launch more non-language courses over time.

"In terms of new courses, we're not announcing new courses at the moment," CEO Luis von Ahn said on the Q3 earnings call. "When we added Chess, already AI helped quite a bit. And we were able to add Chess, idea to being in the app, in about 9 months. We can do that even faster now."

The stock might not follow suit immediately, as Wall Street can be stubborn about both market darlings and underrated winners. But this looks like a great time to start building a Duolingo position for the long haul.

Avvertenza sulle dichiarazioni previsionaliIl presente comunicato stampa contiene dichiarazioni che costituiscono "dichiarazioni previsionali" ai sensi del Private Securities Litigation Reform Act del 1995, e successive modifiche. Tali dichiarazioni previsionali comportano rischi e incertezze significativi che potrebbero determinare risultati effettivi sostanzialmente diversi da quelli espressi o implicitamente indicati. I fattori che potrebbero determinare o contribuire a tali differenze comprendono, a titolo esemplificativo ma non esaustivo: l'estrema volatilitÃ  e imprevedibilitÃ  dei prezzi degli asset digitali, inclusi ETH e Bitcoin; la dipendenza della SocietÃ  da fonti di pricing di terze parti e dai valori di mercato riportati, che possono subire variazioni sostanziali; i cambiamenti delle condizioni di mercato che incidono sul prezzo di negoziazione e sui volumi dei titoli della SocietÃ ; le azioni della SEC, della CFTC e di altri organismi di regolamentazione; i rischi connessi agli investimenti "moonshot" della SocietÃ , inclusi quelli in Eightco Holdings e Beast Industries, e alla valutazione di tali investimenti; Le dichiarazioni previsionali contenute nel presente comunicato stampa si basano sulle informazioni a disposizione del management alla data del presente comunicato e riflettono le attuali aspettative, stime e convinzioni del management in merito a eventi futuri. I risultati effettivi potrebbero differire in misura sostanziale da quelli espressi o impliciti sulla base dei fattori sopra descritti e di quelli indicati nella sezione Risk Factors (Fattori di rischio) dell'Annual Report on Form 10-K della SocietÃ  relativo all'esercizio fiscale conclusosi il 31 agosto 2026, depositato presso la SEC il 10 novembre 2026, nei Quarterly Reports on Form 10-Q della SocietÃ  e negli altri documenti depositati dalla SocietÃ  presso la SEC, come modificati o aggiornati di volta in volta. Bitmine declina espressamente qualsiasi obbligo di aggiornare o rivedere dichiarazioni previsionali, salvo quanto previsto dal diritto applicabile.

Jensen Huang’s return is what Silicon Valley wanted—but things won’t necessarily be the same

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Jensen Huang’s affair with an alleged Chinese spy led to his ouster from Nvidia. 11 days later, he’s back—the outcome Silicon Valley founders, watchers, and luminaries wanted all along.

Within hours of Huang’s original (and ignominious) exit from Nvidia earlier this month, a “Stand With Jensen” petition circulated online, demanding Huang’s return to the company he’d cofounded in 1993. Signatories included Elon Musk, Epic Games CEO Tim Sweeney, Shopify CEO Tobias Lütke, Atreides Management CIO Gavin Baker, and Databricks CEO Ali Ghodsi, who wrote in the comments “Nvidia runs on Jensen! Bring him back!”

Travis Kalanick, Uber’s pugnacious cofounder and former CEO, was both a signatory and especially vocal in his support.

“Jensen is one of the great builders of our time,” Kalanick—himself ousted in 2017 from Uber amid compounding scandals—wrote in a public post on the “Stand With Jensen” site. “Liberty and civilizational progress depend on Jensen,” Kalanick declared. “He has erred, yes—to err is to be human—but I do not believe anything he has done rises to the bar of separating him from his company. Jensen deserves fairness in adjudication and we must presume innocence until that adjudication proves otherwise beyond a reasonable doubt.”

Huang’s return is a Rorschach test of sorts. Some critics see a corporate governance nightmare, while much of the wider public has memed or expressed horror at the circumstances of Huang’s initial resignation (widely described as potentially raising national security concerns). In Silicon Valley though, Huang’s ouster was an injustice that trumped any other aspects of the story . To tech, Huang remains the most indispensable of founders, a nearly god-like figure in a culture that worships “founder mode.”

So, Huang’s return, however questionable it may be, is cause for celebration. Even the very circumspect Sam Altman, who’d previously expressed cautious sympathy for Huang (and who of course, went through a very similar ordeal), tweeted on X on Monday, “Jensen!” Musk, meanwhile, tweeted: “The mistake has been corrected.” Sarah Guo, the plugged-in founder of Conviction VC, welcomed Huang back. The chorus from tech was, in its way, relatively unanimous about just how positive this news was. (The markets agreed vigorously, Nvidia closed yesterday up about 31%, after last week’s massive tech rout.) Meanwhile, for Nvidia’s board, it’s a dicey U-turn.

“Six days after NVIDIA’s board said it had no intention of reinstating Jensen Huang as CEO, he’s back—before the board’s own independent investigation has reported anything, before he answers questions from Congress next week, and with no conditions attached (or at least none that anyone outside the boardroom can see),” said Nicole Bestard, founder of strategic communications firm Quarter Horse Strategies, via email. “From a reputation standpoint, this is a huge mistake. It asks investors, employees, and the public to take the board’s judgment on faith.”

The move from here, in part, is likely to attempt to bury this, David Tepper, CEO and cofounder of Ascerta, predicts.

“It very likely means that Nvidia will be putting out their next GPU cycle (the 6000 series) much sooner than otherwise expected,” he said via email. “They will be eager to bury the news cycle with Nvidia’s continued dominance in the space versus keeping the attention on his infidelity.”

Nevertheless, things are not likely to be the same from here on out, said Umesh Padval, managing partner at Seligman Ventures.

“No one can replace the vision he has,” said Padval. “With him back, he may not go back to 100% immediately, he’ll likely be more cautious. It will still be significantly better than a few weeks back.”

Translation: We still can’t assume Huang will continue to be as aggressive an investor, operator, and guarantor as he may have been before, even with a supposed return to normalcy.

The private markets ecosystem has to stand by itself,” said Padval. “This was driven by Nvidia a lot in the past, the whole AI ecosystem. If participation is less, the venture community needs to step it up with conviction. We all need to believe in the companies we’re backing. And if we believe in the companies, [we] don’t have to wait for the signal from Nvidia.”

Allie Garfinkle is a senior writer and editor at Fortune, where she runs Term Sheet; leads coverage of private capital, investors, and startups; and co-chairs the Brainstorm conference series.

Brandi Passante Reflects On Losing Storage Wars’ Darrell Sheets: ‘It Definitely Changed The Dynamic’

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

DWTS' Glee Tribute Made Me Cry, But What Was Going On In The Background Made Me Irate

OG Mormon Wives Star Mayci Neeley Had A Candid Reaction To Her Texts Being Shown In OC

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

A death that really puts things in perspective.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Warning: There is discussion of suicide in this story.

It’s been seven months since Storage Wars star Darrell Sheets died by suicide in his home in Arizona, and it sounds like the other stars of the show, like Brandi Passante, are still, understandably, processing his death. Season 19 of the reality show kicks off on the 2026 television schedule on November 28th, and ahead of its premiere, Passante talked about Sheets’ passing and how it has affected both her personally and the production of the show.

Sheets left the show as a full-time cast member in 2019 after suffering a heart attack. He remained in contact with the rest of the cast, including Passante, and he made occasional guest appearances after retiring. In a discussion with TV Insider, Passante talked about how his death has affected the show, including how the rest of the cast work with each other:

It definitely changed the dynamic with everybody. We’re all a little softer and kinder to each other since that happened. There was a lot of beef and animosity before between most of us. Some things are not worth fighting.

Sheets’ death, like so many do, really seems to have put things in perspective for the rest of the cast. Their rivalries are what make the show so endlessly watchable, but in the end, they have known each other for almost two decades now, so something like this is bound to change things. Sheets’ manner of death is also a particularly difficult thing to endure, according to Passante:

I’ve endured a lot of deaths in that same manner over the years, and that is just a grief that, you know, kind of sticks with you. It’s not like losing somebody to cancer or a heart attack. It’s something that you think maybe you could have helped prevent. It’s one of those difficult deaths that you never can really wrap your head around.

Anyone who has lost a loved one to suicide knows how Passante feels. It can feel completely helpless. For her part, Passante is doing what she can, including checking in on Sheets’ son, Brandon, who was by his father’s side at all the auctions until Darrell left the show. Passante said:

I talk to [his son] Brandon a lot now. We didn’t really chat much over the years when they left. We would talk every once in a while during holidays or something, but now I just try to keep tabs. He sends me little messages maybe once a week. I just like to check in on him because it’s a hard thing to swallow. I’m sure that he’s missing his dad.

Ultimately, Passante summed it up quite succinctly by saying simply, “It sucks.”

There has been a lot of drama on Storage Wars over the years, and while much of it is contrived for the show, behind-the-scenes has been filled with drama, too. Passante has been in the middle of some of it when she divorced her long-time partner in life and in business, Jarrod Schulz, after Season 13 of the show. There has been nothing like the tragedy that befell Sheets, though, and keeping everything in perspective is important.

Hugh Scott is the Syndication Editor for CinemaBlend. Before CinemaBlend, he was the managing editor for Suggest.com and Gossipcop.com, covering celebrity news and debunking false gossip. He has been in the publishing industry for almost two decades, covering pop culture – movies and TV shows, especially – with a keen interest and love for Gen X culture, the older influences on it, and what it has since inspired. He graduated from Boston University with a degree in Political Science but cured himself of the desire to be a politician almost immediately after graduation.

Steven Pressley answers Dundee resignation question after Hearts thrashing - but no Hibs appeal planned

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Steven Pressley did not consider quitting in the wake of Dundee suffering their biggest-ever home defeat against Hearts on Saturday night.

The 8-0 loss led the manager to take full blame afterwards. He said he wanted to “crawl under a rock and hide” following such a landmark reversal.

Did you know with a Digital Subscription to The Scotsman, you can get unlimited access to the website including our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

The 53-year-old has had little time to ponder his future. Dundee are back in action as soon as Tuesday night at Easter Road against Hibs, who are themselves hurting following a 4-0 home defeat against Dundee United in their last outing. "It has been a tough 24, 48 hours," said Pressley. "There’s a lot of embarrassment, a lot of disappointment."

He stressed there was also a determination to learn from the most challenging experience of his managerial career. Although he has spoken before of coming close to walking away on at least two occasions last season, resigning had not crossed his mind this time.

“Not this weekend, no,” he said. “I've had to find strength from I don't know where, but not this weekend. Not this weekend. But it was hugely embarrassing.

“It probably ranks as the most embarrassing moment of my managerial career thus far.

“I'm 17, 18 years into my coaching career and it's why, in football, you can never become complacent.

“Because nothing ever surprises you. You think you've lived through everything, and then something else comes along. And very much that was Saturday.”

Pressley has admitted considering quitting after disappointing results in his first season at Dundee, one of which was a 4-0 defeat at Pittodrie against Aberdeen. Dundee recovered from a poor start to the campaign and Pressley succeeded in getting his critics firmly on side.

However, the historic nature of Saturday’s result meant he found himself under fire again from some quarters. Pressley has resolved to find the answers. Dundee will be without key defender Owen Bevan, who was sent off on Saturday night, against Hibs.

The club have decided against appealing the decision. “We accept the punishment,” said Pressley. “It is not ideal but I don’t think there was a real chance of it being overturned.”

Pressley suggested there would not be a radical overhaul of the side or system. “It is difficult to change a lot in two days so we have to be very careful," he said. "But we have thought a lot about what we can do. There has to be a change in (the suspended) Owen Bevan. There is a lot to consider. But the most important thing is we give a response and try to get some level of pride back.”

10 Hidden Controls Every 'GTA 6' Player Should Know

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

You can have a ton of fun in GTA 6 even if you only follow the core gameplay loop of running around, stealing cars, and completing missions and side quests. However, if you take a minute to learn a few hidden controls, I think you'll enjoy the game even more. These are fairly simple controls that let you hide from the cops, trash talk other drivers, or flip the bird when you're feeling like annoying NPCs. These are 10 of the best hidden GTA 6 controls that'll help you unlock an entirely new side of the game.

If you just want to sit back and relax as your character drives around Leonida in GTA 6, you should try the auto-drive mode. It's a relaxing way to enjoy the streets without having to worry about following traffic rules. To enable it, you need to get in a vehicle and add a waypoint on the map. This serves as your character's destination, so if you want a longer drive, be sure to drop the waypoint far, far away. Next, hold the down arrow on the D-Pad, and press the Option button. This enables cinematic mode. Then, hold the Circle button to enable auto-drive.

Your character is going to commit a lot of crimes in GTA 6. When this happens, you attract heat, and soon enough, cops will begin chasing you. Switching cars is a great way to reduce the amount of attention you get from law enforcement, but you can still be identified by your face or clothing. Until you can reach a safe spot to swap outfits, maintain a low profile by ducking while driving. Hold the Circle button while driving to duck.

EA's Road Rash was among the first racing games I played as a child, and I remember how much fun it was to kick your rivals while speeding through the game's various courses. You can have a taste of that in GTA 6 too. Press the Square button while you're riding a bike, and your character will kick anyone who's near you. If you do this in a car, it opens the door, and hits people around the car. To annoy other drivers, press R3, and your character will direct some trash talk towards NPCs.

Hold Circle to lean back against walls, or to sit on the beach. To rest on a ledge, you can hold L3. This lets you take a moment to soak in beautiful views in the game, or lounge around as you listen to NPC conversations. Paying attention to those conversations can lead to some fun side quests, and sometimes give you a tip or two for quick money or other rewards.

If you really want to rile people up in GTA 6, you can flip the bird. This works only when your character is unarmed. Hold L2, focus on a person, then press R1. Actions have consequences in GTA 6, so be prepared to fight NPCs when you do this.

When you're being chased by cops, enemies, or angry NPCs, you can use a quick shortcut to look back without turning the camera around. Just hold R3 to look behind you.

While driving, press Circle to toggle headlights (and switch to high beams). This is particularly useful when you're offroading at night. You can also hold left arrow on the D-Pad, and move the right stick up to access other vehicle controls like hazard lights, opening or closing windows, etc., in case you want to add to the realism.

Stealing emergency vehicles in GTA 6 is risky, but the reward is totally worth it. Not only do you find stashes of weapons, gear, and money in some of these vehicles, but you also have access to sirens that help you clear traffic off your path. If you steal an ambulance or a cop car, hold L3, and move the left stick up to go through different siren tones. Press L3 and the left stick up to enable the siren.

GTA 6 is a third-person game, but it has a first-person mode that partially works. It is useful for looking at smaller objects around your character, such as posters, small signs, or to look for clues in and around a house. (Yes, I am talking about Mandy's creepy house and bad vibes.) Hold L2 + hit R3 to switch to first-person mode. Sadly, you can't move when you do this, but you can look around. Try it in Mandy's property to observe a few nasty surprises.

When you sprint towards a car, press the X button to slide across the hood. This is much faster and smoother than running around parked cars or jumping over them. It also looks cool, which is reason enough to try this feature.

Pranay Parab is an independent tech journalist based in Mumbai, India. He covers tech for Lifehacker, and specializes in tutorials and in-depth features.

Billy Ray Joins Oscar Short Contender ‘Magan’s Fare,’ Starring Barkhad Abdi, as Executive Producer

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A “Captain Phillips” reunion is upon the Best Live Action Short Oscar race.

Screenwriter Billy Ray (“The Hunger Games,” “Shattered Glass,” “Richard Jewell,” Nicole Kidman’s AMC ad), who penned the 2013 Oscar-nominated “Captain Phillips, has boarded the film “Magan’s Fare” as an executive producer. The new film stars Barkhad Abdi, the Somali actor who broke out in the Paul Greengrass film and earned a Best Supporting Actor Oscar nomination.

Directed by Christopher Knoblock and Dana White, “Magan’s Fare” is vying in the Best Live Action Short race this year, having qualified out of the Raindance Film Festival where it won the top short prize. Next up, it plays HollyShorts UK. Related Stories Why Is the ‘Avengers: Doomsday’ Trailer So… Gloomy? Internet Erupts Over Latest Look at Marvel Movie AI Film ‘Gods Don’t Give Gifts’ Deemed Ineligible by Academy for Best Animated Feature Oscar

The 26-minute drama stars Abdi as Magan, “a Somali-born rideshare driver who takes an older woman, Jorani (Alexis Rhee), back to her nursing home, only to discover the facility has given away her bed. With his passenger suddenly displaced and no one else willing to take responsibility for her, Magan must figure out what to do next,” per the announcement.

“The last two years have demonstrated on a grand level that small businesses need more help than big ones do,” Ray said at a recent Los Angeles screening of the film. “Well, in our community, small movies need more help than big ones do. I’m happy to be helping this small movie. I hope all of you will too. It’s so beautifully written, it’s so beautifully directed, it’s so beautifully performed. The music, the cinematography, all of it, just executed with such love and care, and integrity.”

Knoblock and White added, “We are deeply honored to have Billy Ray join our ‘Magan’s Fare’ FYC campaign as an executive producer. Billy is a remarkable storyteller whose writing creates complex, deeply human characters that stay with you long after the story ends. His work is an inspiration to us, and we’re especially excited by the opportunity to bring Billy and Barkhad Abdi back together through a film that speaks to the power of compassion and our responsibility to one another. Having Billy’s support means a great deal to us and to the heart of this film.”
